Not run: Maven, the JUnit classes, any window, MariaDB. **Problem 10 of the check (the age shown for the second place of the backups)** was not reproduced by reading: `SecondBackupFolder.catchUp` stores the modified time of the newest backup file, not the time of the check; if the old test backup was made only by giving the file an old name, its modified time is still today, which would show "hôm nay".

**Session 117 (2026-10-24): the 11 problems of the check of 3.48, 4.18 and 4.35 were examined; ten were real and are fixed, one was already fixed.** (1) `TableDAO.add/update/remove` call `Permissions.requireAdmin`; (2) the cashier's home map (`employee-trangchu.fxml`) is in a `ScrollPane` without fixed rows, like the other two; (3) the 200-table limit is checked in `TableDAO.add`; (4) the name check compares the stored and the shown names ("Bàn 5" = "Table 5") and the accents in one form (`TextNormalizer.key`), in the box and again in the DAO, which also checks the seats; (5) `OrderDAO.insert` refuses a new open order on a table that no longer exists (`order.table.gone`); (6) changing only the seats of a starter table keeps its stored name (`TableRules.nameToStore`); (7) the last table cannot be removed (`TableDAO.remove`, atomic in the DELETE, and the dialog); (8) an empty table map shows a line for the administrator or for staff (dashboards and POS); (9) two tills adding at once: the number is chosen again up to three times, then a plain message (`tablemgr.fail.busy`); on SQLite the second till gets "busy", on MariaDB a duplicate key, both are caught; (10) **not valid in session 116**: `markSuccess` already stores the backup file's own time (a 10-day-old file gives OLD and "10 ngày trước", and a second `catchUp()` does not refresh it); regression tests added; (11) the `C:\Lunavera` example of `app.cloud.message` is reworded in both languages (no drive letter), `I18nTest` passes. 1091 `@Test` in 164 classes. Run here on real JavaFX 11, the project's SQLite driver 3.46.1.3 and real JUnit 4: 1091 tests, 6 failures, none in this work (see `progress.md`). Not run: Maven, any MariaDB test, any screen.

**Session 113 (2026-10-25): 3.21 + 4.19 (a reorder level per product), 3.25 (`points_earned`), 3.17 (cash received and change), 4.31 (bank reference) and 5.31 (order of categories and products) were done.** By the boxes: 287 ticked / 54 open (341 numbered items); 1116 `@Test` in 169 test classes. Seven new columns, all added the way the codebase adds them (`SqliteSchema.LATER_COLUMNS` for SQLite and for an existing MariaDB, the `CREATE TABLE` of `MariaDbSchema` for a new one, the importer's column lists): `categories.sort_order`, `products.sort_order`, `products.reorder_level`, `orders.points_earned`, `orders.cash_received`, `orders.change_given`, `orders.transfer_ref`. Every new column is null or 0 for old rows, so nothing changes for a shop until it uses a feature. **A latent bug found on the way and fixed:** the old money migration (`MoneyMigration`, only for a SQLite file from before version 1) rebuilds `products` and `orders` with a fixed column list, which dropped any column added earlier in the same start (it already lost `order_items.unit_cost`); `DatabaseConfig` now adds the later columns a second time after it (`addLaterColumns`, tested in `MoneyMigrationTest`). **Run here:** the new logic tests (29: `PaymentDetailsTest`, `DisplayOrderTest`, `ProductReorderTest`, `ReceiptPaymentExtrasTest`), with all earlier runnable tests 133 logic tests pass and the 57 structural tests pass except the known `app.cloud.message`; the SQL of the new queries on a real SQLite engine; every non-JavaFX file of `src/main` compiles. **Not run:** Maven, the new database tests (`OrderPaymentDetailsTest`, `SalesScreenOrderTest`, `ProductReorderLevelDaoTest`, `LaterColumnsExistTest`, the new `MoneyMigrationTest` case), anything on MariaDB, every JavaFX class (the controllers were reviewed by reading their diffs).

## 1. Requested by the owner (2026-10-10)

### 1.1 Fix the receipt QR so scanning shows readable text  — priority: high, small — **DONE in code (session 35), waiting for a phone scan**
- [x] The scan result looked like jumbled, "encrypted" text. Fixed: the QR text is now ASCII only (see the checked steps below). **Still to do by the owner: scan a new receipt on the phone and tick the last box.**
- Likely cause **(inferred, verify by scanning)**: `ReceiptQr.payload()` builds text with Vietnamese letters and the đ sign
  ("Hóa đơn", "Tổng", "25.000 đ"). `QrCode` writes it as UTF-8 bytes but adds no character-set marker (no ECI segment).
  Many phone scanners then read the bytes as Latin-1 and show garbage such as `HÃ³a Ä‘Æ¡n`. The QR itself is valid; the
  text is just decoded in the wrong character set.
- Fix plan:
  - [ ] Owner: scan a **new** receipt (after this change) on the same phone and scanner app that showed the jumbled text and write down what it shows. (I could not scan with a phone. What I did: decoded the QR image with OpenCV, and showed that the old text read as Latin-1 gives exactly the kind of garbage described, so the cause is very likely this one; a phone result closes it.)
  - [x] Make the payload ASCII-only (for example `Lunavera Coffee` / `Hoa don #12` / `Tong: 25.000 VND` / `14:30 10/10/2026`),
        since plain ASCII reads the same on every scanner. Optional extra: add a UTF-8 ECI marker to `QrCode` too.
  - [x] Keep it a plain summary. It must not be a payment request (see 1.4).
  - [x] Add a test that the payload contains only ASCII characters (`ReceiptQrTest`: ASCII only, one byte per character, amount format, locale independent). Decoding is covered by `QrCodeTest`; I also checked the rendered image with OpenCV by hand.
- Files: `util/ReceiptQr.java`, `util/QrCode.java`, `controller/HoaDonController.java` (draws the image).

### 1.2 Rename uploaded product images to a unique name  — priority: medium, small — **DONE (session 36); confirmed working on Windows by the owner, 2026-10-10**
- [x] Before: `ImageStorage.storeNewFile` named the copy `System.currentTimeMillis() + "_" + originalName` and copied with
      `REPLACE_EXISTING` **(confirmed)**. Two uploads in the same millisecond with the same original name could silently
      overwrite one another, and the original file name (spaces, Vietnamese letters, very long names) was kept in the name.
- What was done:
  - [x] The stored file is now named `<timestamp>-<random UUID>.<extension>`, for example `1760100000000-3f2b8c1e-....jpg`. The original name is dropped.
  - [x] Only the extensions `png`, `jpg` and `jpeg` are accepted (any capitalisation; stored in lowercase). Anything else (`.gif`, `.webp`, `.exe`, no extension, a trailing dot) *(Outdated since bug 2.58: `.jfif`, `.jpe`, `.gif` and `.bmp` are accepted too, JFIF/JPE stored as `.jpg`, 8 MB limit, see 2.58 and 3.12.)*
        is refused **before** a byte is copied, with the message "Chỉ hỗ trợ ảnh PNG hoặc JPG". The product dialog already shows that message.
  - [x] The copy never replaces an existing file (it fails with "file already exists" instead). If the generated name is taken it tries a new one, up to 5 times, then gives up with an error.
  - [x] Old files and old-style names (`1728500000000_latte da.png`, old absolute paths, `seed_*.png` from the starter menu) still resolve; nothing was renamed or moved. The starter-menu seeding was left alone (it uses fixed `seed_<name>` names on purpose, so a re-run reuses them).
  - [x] The product dialog now shows the name of the file you picked, because the stored name is a random string and would mean nothing to the person using the dialog.
  - [x] Tests (`ImageStorageTest`, 6 new, 12 in total): two uploads of the same file give two names and two files; the original name does not appear in the stored name (plain, accented and 150-character names);
        only PNG/JPEG are accepted; a forced name collision does not overwrite the existing file and retries with a new name; the class gives up when every name is taken; an old-style name still resolves.
        I ran these 12 here with a small stand-in test runner (no Maven access); they all passed. Run `mvn test` on your machine to confirm with real JUnit.
- Owner check: [x] on Windows, a new picture gets a name like `<number>-<letters and digits>.jpg` and still shows in the table. **Confirmed working, 2026-10-10.**
- Not changed here (tracked elsewhere): the old picture is still deleted at the moment a new one is picked, before Save (bug 2.21), and the Vietnamese-letter test names are skipped on a machine whose file system cannot create them.
- Files: `util/ImageStorage.java`, `ImageStorageTest.java`, the product dialog in `AdminController` (label only).

### 1.3 Keep the data inside the project instead of the user's home folder  — priority: high, medium — **DONE in code (session 37), waiting for the owner's check**
- [x] Before: the database, pictures, logs and backups lived in `~/.lunavera-coffee` (`C:\Users\<name>\.lunavera-coffee` on Windows) **(confirmed)**, written as separate `user.home` paths in five classes.
- What was done:
  - [x] **One place decides where the data is:** the new `util/DataPaths`. The data folder is, in this order: the `-Dlunavera.dataDir=...` system property, the `LUNAVERA_DATA_DIR` environment variable, otherwise **`data/` inside the folder the app is started from** (the project folder with `mvn javafx:run`). A relative value counts from the start-up folder. A blank value is ignored.
        `DatabaseConfig`, `ImageStorage`, `AppLogging` and `MoneyMigration` all ask (and `DataCleanup`, removed in session 40) `DataPaths` now; nothing else builds a path by hand. The folders are `data/lunavera.db`, `data/images/`, `data/logs/`, `data/backups/`.
  - [x] **`data/` is in `.gitignore`**, with a comment, so the database (customer phones, password hashes) cannot be committed by mistake.
  - [x] **One-time copy from the old folder** (`DataPaths.migrateLegacyDataIfNeeded`, called before the first database connection): if the new folder has no database yet and `~/.lunavera-coffee/lunavera.db` exists, the database (copied to a temp name, then renamed, so a half-copied file never counts), the `images/` and the `backups/` are **copied**; the old folder is never changed or deleted. Existing files in the new folder are never overwritten. A line in the log says what was copied.
        Logs are not copied (history, not data). A marker file `data/.migrated-from-home` records the move, so deleting `data/lunavera.db` later to start fresh does **not** quietly bring the old data back.
  - [x] **If the old database cannot be copied** (disk full, permissions), the app stops with a clear message instead of starting with an empty database that would look like all the shop's data was lost; the old data is untouched. (Pictures and backups that fail to copy are only logged: copy them by hand.)
  - [x] **Old picture paths keep working:** products saved with a full path to the old folder still show, because that folder is left in place.
  - [x] **Backups:** the save dialog now opens in your user folder, not in `data/`; saving a backup inside `data/` shows a warning (a backup next to the live data is not a real backup); and the success message names the real database file instead of `~/.lunavera-coffee` (this also fixes bug 2.28).
  - [x] **Backing up onto the live database file is refused** (bug 2.23, first part), because the database now sits in a folder that is easy to browse to; see the update under bug 2.23.
  - [x] Tests: `DataPathsTest` (12: setting, relative setting, default, blank setting, "inside the data folder", copy of database/pictures/backups with the old folder untouched, runs only once, deleting the new database does not bring the old one back, never overwrites an existing database, nothing to copy, existing pictures kept, new folder = old folder) and `BackupSafetyTest` (3: the live file is refused and the database stays intact, a backup to another file is a valid database, the database is in the configured folder).
        All database tests now point `lunavera.dataDir` (and `user.home`) at a temp folder, so no test can touch your real `data/` or read your real old folder. I ran `DataPathsTest` here (12 passed) with a stand-in test runner; the database tests need SQLite and Maven, so they are **not run here**.
- Still to do by the owner: [ ] the two checks in section 0 (first start from the project folder, then the backup dialog).
- Things to know:
  - The default `data/` is relative to where the program is **started**. `mvn javafx:run` starts in the project folder, so it works. A packaged build or an IDE run configuration may start somewhere else: set `-Dlunavera.dataDir="D:\LuneveraData"` (or the environment variable) for those, and the data will not move when you rebuild.
  - Cleaning (`mvn clean`), switching git branches and re-extracting the zip do not delete `data/` as long as you extract the new zip **over** the old project folder, or copy `data/` into the new one. Extracting into a new empty folder starts a new `data/` (the old one is in the old project folder, and the original data is still in `~/.lunavera-coffee`). This is written down in the new `README.md`.
  - The backup button (and the automatic backups in `data/backups/`) still keep copies on the same disk. Copy backups to a USB stick or another machine regularly (see 2A.4 and 6.9).
  - The pictures of the starter menu are stored with fixed names, so a re-run reuses them (unchanged by this item).
- Not done here (tracked elsewhere): backup bug 2.23 other parts (tmp-then-rename, `.db` extension), the quiet fallback when the folder cannot be created (2.26), restore from the app (4.1).
- Files: `util/DataPaths.java` (new), `config/DatabaseConfig.java`, `util/ImageStorage.java`, `util/AppLogging.java`, `config/MoneyMigration.java`, `AdminController` (backup dialog), `.gitignore`, `README.md` (new), `DataPathsTest`, `BackupSafetyTest`, and the tests that set `user.home`.

### 1.4 Bank transfer / bank card payment with a QR image named `qr-transfer`  — priority: medium, medium — **DONE in code (session 38) except the picture itself: the owner must supply the QR image**
- [ ] **Owner: send the shop's transfer QR picture** and say which kind it is. The code never makes up a QR (a wrong one would send customers' money to the wrong account), so until the picture exists the dialog says it is missing.
      Save it as `qr-transfer.png` (or `.jpg` / `.jpeg`, any capitalisation) in the **`data/`** folder next to `pom.xml`. No rebuild is needed; it is read each time the payment dialog is opened.
      Optional fallback: put it in `src/main/resources/images/qr-transfer.png` to ship it with the program (used only when `data/` has none).
- What was done:

### 1.5 A way to edit the database directly (like phpMyAdmin), and maybe another database  — priority: medium — **DONE in code (sessions 42-44): MariaDB/MySQL is now the app's default database (phpMyAdmin works on the shop's data), SQLite is an option and an hourly snapshot copy; waiting for the owner's check on a real server**
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-11): option C, MariaDB/MySQL with phpMyAdmin.** Options A (an external SQLite tool) and B (a built-in "Database" page) stay discarded.
- **Assumptions I made because the owner's questions were not answered (tell me if any is wrong):** MariaDB (a MySQL server works with the same driver and SQL); one server on the shop PC, with every till pointing at it by host name; a Windows install with XAMPP is acceptable (the guide is written for it); no connection pool yet (see "Not done").
- **How it is switched (changed in session 44):** MariaDB/MySQL is the **default**. With no `data/database.properties` the app looks for a server on this computer (`127.0.0.1:3306`, database `lunavera`) and, if there is none, stops at start-up with a message that says where to put the settings file (and that `db.type=sqlite` brings the old file back). `mvn test` is unaffected: the pom makes the tests run on SQLite unless `-Dlunavera.test.db=mariadb` is given.
  Earlier (sessions 42-43) SQLite was the default; the owner decided on 2026-10-13 to make MariaDB the default and keep SQLite as an option plus an hourly snapshot copy. The SQL that differs is in one small class (`SqlDialect`), and tests compare the two table definitions column by column so they cannot drift apart unnoticed.
- What was done:
  - [x] **Phase 1, the seam.** `DatabaseSettings` reads `data/database.properties` (host, port, name, user, password, optional SQLite import path, optional `mysqldump` path; every key can also be a `-Dlunavera.<key>` option; names and hosts are checked so nothing odd can reach the URL; the password is never written to a log or a message). `DatabaseConfig` connects per database, skips the SQLite-only `PRAGMA`s for MariaDB, and keeps the schema version in a `schema_version` table instead of `PRAGMA user_version`.
        Driver `org.mariadb.jdbc:mariadb-java-client:3.4.1` in `pom.xml` and `requires org.mariadb.jdbc` in `module-info.java`. If the server is down the start-up message now says so and names the settings file (`StartupProblem`).
  - [x] **Phase 2, the SQL.** Every item of the list in the old plan was handled: `AUTOINCREMENT` (a separate MariaDB table definition, `MariaDbSchema`: `INT` ids, `BIGINT` whole-dong money, real `DATETIME`/`DATE`, `VARCHAR` lengths, InnoDB, utf8mb4, indexes and the unique customer phone key);
        `DATE('now','localtime')` and `DATE('now','-6 days',...)` (the dashboard asks with a date from the app's own clock, which also removes any difference between the server's and the till's time zone); `VACUUM INTO` (becomes `mysqldump`); `INSERT OR IGNORE` and `COLLATE NOCASE` (spelled by `SqlDialect`); `MAX(0, x)` (a `CASE`); `PRAGMA user_version`, `table_info`, `sqlite_master`, `foreign_keys`, `busy_timeout` (`schema_version`, `information_schema`, nothing needed).
        Dates go through one helper, `DbTime` (ISO text in SQLite as before, `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss` for MariaDB; reading accepts both).
        **Other differences found while converting (not in the old list):** (1) `LIKE ... ESCAPE '\'` means something else in MySQL, which reads a backslash inside quotes as its own escape: the history search now uses `!` as the escape character in both databases (`escapeLike` and its test changed); (2) `CAST(x AS TEXT)` became `CAST(x AS CHAR)` (works in both); (3) a plain `TEXT` column cannot be a key in MySQL, so the small flags table and the unique columns use `VARCHAR`;
        (4) the unique customer phone from bug 2.7 is a partial index in SQLite, which MySQL does not have: in MariaDB mode a customer without a phone is stored as **NULL** (never `""`), which a plain unique key allows to repeat; `CustomerDAO` does that for both databases and recognises MariaDB's duplicate-key error 1062; (5) `SchemaMigrations` (indexes, unique phone) and `MoneyMigration` run only on SQLite, because a new MariaDB database is created with all of that.
  - [x] **Phase 3, moving the data and backing up.** `SqliteToMariaDbImporter`: put `db.import.sqlite=data/lunavera.db` in the settings file and the next start copies **everything** into the **empty** MariaDB database, keeping every id, converting the dates, turning blank customer phones into NULL, and checking that every table has the same number of rows. It is one transaction (any failure rolls everything back and the app stops with a message), it never runs if MariaDB already has data, and the SQLite file is only read.
        Backups: the "Sao lưu dữ liệu" zip and the daily automatic backup hold `lunavera.sql` (a `mysqldump`, the password sent in an environment variable, not on the command line) plus the pictures. **Restore is by hand** with phpMyAdmin's Import (the in-app "Khôi phục dữ liệu" is SQLite-only and now says how, instead of swapping a file the app would ignore).
  - [x] **Phase 4, the guide.** `docs/DATABASE.md` (install XAMPP, create the database and a user that is **not** root, the settings file, the import, rules for editing in phpMyAdmin, backups and restore, security, what each error means, the differences from SQLite, running the tests on MariaDB), `database.properties.example`, and the README. `data/` was already in `.gitignore`, so the password file is outside git.
  - [x] **Session 43 follow-up (what was left over).**
        (1) **"Mở phpMyAdmin" button** on the admin dashboard, shown only to an admin when the shop runs on MariaDB: it asks first (phpMyAdmin skips the app's checks: stock, loyalty points, order totals) and then opens `http://localhost/phpmyadmin/` in the browser; `db.phpmyadmin` changes the address, and only `http://` / `https://` addresses are accepted (a `file:` or `javascript:` link is refused when the settings are read).
        (2) **Plain Vietnamese messages when the database has a problem you can act on** (`DataAccessException`): server unreachable, wrong login (names `data/database.properties`), database busy. Before this, a stopped server in the middle of a sale showed only "Failed to ...". The technical text stays in brackets; every other error is unchanged.
        (3) **Numbered upgrades for an existing MariaDB database** (`MariaDbMigrations`, driven by `schema_version`; no step is needed today). It also **refuses to start an app that is older than the database**, so an old copy cannot damage newer tables.
        (4) **An SQL audit of every remaining query for MySQL 8:** one real problem found and fixed: the table list (`TableDAO.findAll`) selected a column that was not in its `GROUP BY`, which SQLite and MariaDB accept but MySQL 8 refuses; it now uses `MAX(id)` (the newest open order of a table). No other query needed a change.
        (5) **Bug 2.64 fixed** (the money migration of an old SQLite file no longer drops `table_number` and `discount_reason`); see that item.
  - [x] **Session 44: the owner's decisions of 2026-10-13, and the first real build.**
        **First real build (owner's `mvn clean test`):** it compiled with the MariaDB driver on the module path, which **confirms the module name `org.mariadb.jdbc` and the driver version**; 305 tests ran, 304 passed, and 1 failed: `FontBundleTest` (`java.awt.Font` could not be read: "module does not read java.desktop"). That test is from the font work (1.6), not from the database work; **fixed** by letting the test JVM (only) read `java.desktop` (`--add-reads` in the surefire `argLine`). The pom also pins surefire at 3.6.0, the version Maven was already using, which removes the "plugin version is missing" warning.
        **Carrying the old data over (owner's choice, 2026-10-13: "carry my old data over").** The import (`db.import.sqlite=data/lunavera.db`, now enabled in `database.properties.example`) works **at any time while MariaDB holds nothing but what the app creates by itself on a first start** (the `admin` account and its employee row, the 12 dining tables, the starter menu): then it clears those and brings the old data in, in one transaction, so the first start no longer closes the door on it.
        It is refused (log line, nothing changed) when MariaDB already has **real data**: any customer, any order, a second account or employee, or a product or category that is not part of the starter menu. A file without a `users` table is ignored with a warning before anything is touched. The old accounts come over, so you sign in with the **old** admin password. If it fails, everything is rolled back; the usual cause is two customers with the same phone (MariaDB allows one per phone), and the message names the number and says to fix or merge them in the SQLite version of the app.
        Not changed: the old `data/lunavera.db` (only read) and `data/images` (the pictures stay where they are).
        **MariaDB is the default** (above). **Hourly SQLite snapshots (`SqliteSnapshotService`):** while the shop runs on MariaDB the app copies every table into `data/snapshots/lunavera-yyyyMMdd-HHmmss.db` every hour it is open (the first about 30 seconds after start-up if the newest is older than an hour) and keeps only the **newest 10** (older ones are deleted; files that are not snapshots are never touched). One consistent view of the data (a `START TRANSACTION WITH CONSISTENT SNAPSHOT`), a row-count check per table, written under a `.partial` name and renamed when complete, a failure logged and retried an hour later, never stopping the app. The file is a complete SQLite database at the current version with its indexes, so it can also be used by the app itself (copy it over `data/lunavera.db`, `db.type=sqlite`); it holds no pictures. A SQLite shop gets no snapshots.
  - [x] **Tests on a real server are possible and safe.** `mvn test -Dlunavera.test.db=mariadb -Dlunavera.test.db.password=...` runs every database test on a MariaDB database whose name **must end in `_test`** (it drops every table in it first; any other name is refused, so the shop's database can never be wiped). Tests about SQLite files, PRAGMAs and the SQLite-only migrations skip themselves in that mode.
  - [x] **New tests, 57 (42 in session 42, 15 in session 43), the pure ones run here and passed:** session 43 added `DataAccessExceptionTest` (5), `MariaDbMigrationsTest` (6), two in `DatabaseSettingsTest` (the phpMyAdmin address) and two in `MoneyMigrationTest` (these two need SQLite, so they are compiled but not run here; I replayed their SQL in Python and it behaved). Session 42: `DatabaseSettingsTest` (10), `SqlDialectTest` (5), `DbTimeTest` (7), `MariaDbSchemaTest` (8: no SQLite-only words, tables created after the ones they point to, drop order, **same columns as the SQLite tables**, money is `BIGINT`, dates are `DATETIME`, login names exact, one phone per customer), `SqliteToMariaDbImporterTest` (6: the copied tables and columns equal the MariaDB tables, copy order is valid, date and blank-phone conversion), `MariaDbBackupTest` (2: the command, the password never on it), `BackupEntryNameTest` (3), and one in `StartupProblemTest`. The existing `escapeLike` test was changed for the new escape character.
- **Not done / limits (please read):**

The first real start may show a typo or a rule I did not foresee: send me the exact message.

Two tills editing the same open order would end with the last save.

### 1.6 Fix the text font so it looks right on Windows (it only looks right on Linux today)  — priority: high — **done in code in session 39 (one bundled font on every window); waiting for the owner's Windows check**
- [x] Reported by the owner: the text font works on Linux but not on Windows. **Cause confirmed from the owner's screenshots (below): Georgia has no glyphs for many Vietnamese letters.**
- **DONE in session 39 (code):** the app now bundles **Be Vietnam Pro** (regular, italic, bold; SIL Open Font License, `resources/fonts/OFL.txt`) and every window uses it.
  - New `util/AppStyle`: loads the three font files and adds `css/app.css` (`.root { -fx-font-family: "Be Vietnam Pro"; }`) to the scene of **every** window that opens: screens, the code-built windows, all dialogs and alerts, menus and tooltips. Called once in `HelloApplication.start`. So the dialogs and the receipt window are covered without touching each one.
  - Every `-fx-font-family` (about 130 rules: Georgia, Serif, System, Segoe UI) was removed from the 7 style sheets, so the text inherits the one font. The chart tick labels name the font too.
  - `font-weight: 600` (25 places) became `bold` and `500` (4 places) became `normal`, because only regular, italic and bold are bundled and the medium/semi-bold files are separate font families to JavaFX. This makes semi-bold headings a little heavier.
  - **Checked here:** I ran real JavaFX with a virtual screen and a screenshot: the font loads, the screen, a button, an alert, bold and italic all resolve to Be Vietnam Pro, and `ẮẰẲẴẶ ỘỢỮỰ ỹỵ đĐ` and the digits draw correctly (the digit 0 is now a normal zero). `FontBundleTest` (4 tests) checks the font files hold every Vietnamese letter with every tone mark and that no style sheet names a machine-dependent font again.
  - **Not verified:** Windows itself. The icon symbols (`⌂ ▤ ▦ ● ☕ ✎ ✓ ← →` and others, see 2.34) are not in this font, so Windows has to borrow them from another font; on Linux that works. See the check in section 0. If an icon shows as a square, give those labels `Segoe UI Symbol`.
  - Left for later: 125% scaling check (bug 2.4), and the decision about replacing the text icons with drawn shapes (2.34).
- **Evidence from the owner's Windows screenshots (2026-10-10, 6 screens): the cause is confirmed.** On the **products, categories and accounts** screens every Vietnamese letter that carries a hook, a dot below or a stacked mark is drawn as an empty square, while the **dashboard, orders and customers** screens look right.
  - Broken (empty squares): `ả ạ ấ ầ ẩ ậ ẻ ẹ ế ề ệ ỉ ị ỏ ọ ố ồ ộ ớ ụ ủ ứ ừ ự` and also `ư` (examples seen: "Qu□n lý s□n ph□m" for "Quản lý sản phẩm", "T□t c□" for "Tất cả", "N□□c ép" for "Nước ép", "Ch□a ch□n" for "Chưa chọn", "Tr□ng thái" for "Trạng thái", "Bánh ng□t" for "Bánh ngọt", "Danh m□c" for "Danh mục").
    These are the letters from the Unicode block "Latin Extended Additional" (U+1EA0-U+1EF9) plus `ư`/`ơ` (U+01A0-U+01B0).
  - Fine on the same screens: `à á â ã è é ê ì í ò ó ô õ ù ú ý ă đ Đ` ("Cà phê", "Trà", "Đang bán", "Tên" all show correctly), so only part of Vietnamese is missing, which is why it looks like a random square here and there.
  - Which screens: exactly the three whose style sheets use **Georgia**: `quanlysanpham.css`, `quanlydanhmuc.css`, `quanlytaikhoan.css`. The screens that use "Segoe UI" or the default font (`admin-trangchu.css`, `quanlydonhang.css`, and the customers screen, which uses the dashboard sheet) show every letter. The sidebar (always Segoe UI) is correct even on the broken screens.
  - Cause: **the Georgia that ships with Windows does not contain these Vietnamese letters**, and JavaFX does not borrow them from another font. On Linux "Georgia" is not installed, so a default font with full Vietnamese coverage is used instead, which is why nobody saw it there.
  - A second, smaller effect visible in the same screenshots: in Georgia the digit 0 is drawn like a small letter "o" (old-style figures): the "0" in the statistics cards, the product counts and `48.000đ`. It does not break anything, but numbers look uneven.
- **Quick stopgap (about 10 minutes, then check each screen):** replace `Georgia` and `Serif` with `"Segoe UI"` in the four style sheets that use them (the three above plus `dangnhap.css` for the login screen), keeping `sans-serif` as the fallback. This fixes Windows at once (Segoe UI is shown correctly in all screenshots) but changes the look of those screens from serif to sans. The proper fix below makes both systems identical.
- What the code says today **(confirmed by reading the style sheets)**: fonts are chosen only by name, and the names differ by screen, so each machine picks whatever it has:
  - `"Segoe UI", sans-serif` — about 56 rules, the dashboards (`admin-trangchu.css`, `employee-trangchu.css`, `quanlydonhang.css`)
  - `"Georgia"` — about 55 rules, the login and the category, product and account screens
  - `"Serif"` and `"System"` — about 20 more rules, mixed in with the above
  - No font file is bundled (no `Font.loadFont`, no `resources/fonts`). Dialogs, alerts and the receipt have no style sheet, so they use the system default font.
  - On Linux neither Georgia nor Segoe UI is normally installed, so every name falls back to one default font and the whole app looks the same. On Windows the real fonts exist, so each screen gets a different typeface.
- Other things to keep in mind (not seen in the screenshots):
  - Screens mix Georgia (serif) and Segoe UI (sans), so the app looks like two different programs.
  - Symbols used as icons (bug 2.34) come from whichever font Windows picks; in the screenshots they show, but small.
  - The login screen, the employee dashboard, the dialogs and the printed receipt were not in the screenshots: check them too after the change (the login sheet uses Georgia, so it is probably affected).
- Plan (decide the typeface first):
  - [x] (assumed, not confirmed by the owner: one sans-serif, Be Vietnam Pro) Owner: choose the look: one sans-serif for everything (suggested: "Be Vietnam Pro", made for Vietnamese, or "Noto Sans"), or keep a serif for headings only. Both are free to ship (SIL Open Font License); check the licence text is included.
  - [x] Bundle the font files in `src/main/resources/fonts/` (regular, medium, bold) and load them once at start-up in `HelloApplication` with `Font.loadFont(...)`, so Windows and Linux use the same file.
  - [x] Replace the font rules in all 7 style sheets with one shared rule, for example a root rule `.root { -fx-font-family: "Be Vietnam Pro"; }` in one shared `app.css` that every screen loads, with `sans-serif` as the fallback. Remove the per-rule `Georgia`, `Segoe UI`, `Serif` and `System` names.
  - [x] Give dialogs, alerts and the receipt window the same style sheet (`AlertUtil.configure` and `SceneNavigator.loadAndShowNewWindow`), so they match the rest.
  - [x] (on Linux, and by the font tests) Check Vietnamese text with all marks (for example `Ắ Ằ Ẳ Ẵ Ặ Ộ Ợ Ữ Ự ỹ ỵ đ Đ`) in the regular, medium and bold files, in tables, buttons, dialogs and the printed receipt.
  - [ ] Decide what to do about icons at the same time (bug 2.34): either keep a symbols font in `resources/fonts/` too, or switch the icons to small SVG shapes so they no longer depend on a font.
  - [ ] Test on a real Windows machine at 100% and 125% scaling.
- Files: `resources/css/*.css` (7 files), a new `resources/css/app.css`, `resources/fonts/`, `HelloApplication.java`, `AlertUtil.java`, `SceneNavigator.java`.

### 1.7 Switch the language between Vietnamese and English  — priority: medium, large (do in phases)
- **Status of this branch (language-switch branch, started from `Lunavera-Coffee-main_small-practices.zip`, sha256 `2c3dfa21...0fea`): Phase 1 DONE in code, Phase 2 and 3 started.** **Under Maven (the owner's `./mvnw -B clean test`): everything compiles and 562 tests pass, 0 failures, 1 skipped (needs MariaDB), including `I18nTest`, `UntranslatedTextTest` and `FxmlControllerCheckTest`. Seen on a screen by the owner (after putting `db.type=sqlite` in `data/database.properties`): the login screen and the home (dashboard) screen show in English, English is still selected after a restart, and the switch looks right.** Pop-ups and the other screens go back to Vietnamese: expected, they are not translated yet (see the untranslated baseline). The owner accepts the switch being on the login screen only for now.

### 1.8 Restore a backup from inside the app when the shop runs on MariaDB  — priority: medium, medium-large — **NEW (session 45), not started**
- Today "Khôi phục dữ liệu" works only on SQLite (it swaps the database file at the next start). On MariaDB the button shows the phpMyAdmin steps (`BackupService.MARIADB_RESTORE_HELP`): unzip `lunavera.sql`, import it in phpMyAdmin, copy `images` back into `data/images`. A manual restore under stress is where mistakes happen, so the owner asked for it to be tracked.
- What it needs: (a) read `lunavera.sql` from the zip and run it against the shop's database through the `mysql` client (like `mysqldump` is used for backups; `db.mysql` path setting), or a pure-JDBC runner for the statements `MariaDbBackup` writes; (b) a safety dump (`before-restore-*.zip`) before anything is changed; (c) all-or-nothing: restore into a scratch database first, check row counts, then swap, or drop and re-create inside one controlled step; (d) pictures restored the same way as on SQLite (swap last); (e) the "restore pending" banner and the cancel button either do not apply (restore happens immediately, after a clear confirmation) or are kept as on SQLite; (f) refuse a dump made by a newer schema version (`schema_version` table); (g) only an admin may do it, and it is logged (`SecurityLog`); (h) tests on a real server (`_test` database) for the success path, a damaged file and a newer version.
- Risks: it overwrites live data on a server that other tills may be using (stop them first, or refuse while another session is active); the SQL file is not checked by the app today. Until this is done the manual steps in `docs/DATABASE.md` section 6 are the way.

### 1.9 MariaDB: account names are only protected by the app, not by the database  — priority: low, small — **NEW (session 45, found while fixing the staff phone), DONE in code (session 115)**
- On SQLite a unique index (`ux_users_username_nocase`) makes `Admin` and `admin` the same account for the database itself. On MariaDB `users.username` is `utf8mb4_bin` (exact), so the database would accept both; only the app's checks (login compares lower-case, `AccountService` refuses a name that exists in any case) stop it, so editing the `users` table in phpMyAdmin, or two tills creating the same name at the same moment, could still create a pair.
- Fix options: a generated lower-case column with a unique key, or switch the column to a case-insensitive collation (which also ignores accents, so `an` and `ản` would collide, probably acceptable for login names). Needs a duplicate check first (skip with a warning, like the SQLite index), a migration for existing MariaDB databases, and a test on a real server.
- **DONE (session 115):** the first option. `users.username_key` is a stored generated column `LOWER(username)` (`utf8mb4_bin`) with the unique key `ux_users_username_key`, in `MariaDbSchema` for a new database and added by `SchemaMigrations.ensureMariaLoginNameKey` to an older one (the column first, then the key; if two names already differ only in capitals the key is skipped with a warning that gives the query to list them, and it is tried again at every start, like the SQLite rule and the staff-phone key). `username` itself stays `utf8mb4_bin`, so `mai` and `mái` are still different logins (the collation option would have merged them). The app's own check and message are unchanged: the server's `Duplicate entry ... for key 'ux_users_username_key'` contains "username", so `AccountService.translate` shows "tên đăng nhập đã có". Run for real on MariaDB 10.11 here (the DDL, the pair `Mai`/`mai`/`MAI` refused, `mái` allowed, the upgrade path with and without an existing pair, and a `mariadb-dump` / reload round trip); the JUnit class `MariaDbLoginNameKeyTest` (2 tests) runs in the `test-mariadb` CI job and was not run here (no MariaDB JDBC driver). `MariaDbSchemaTest` knows `username_key` is MariaDB-only. Not done: nothing needed on SQLite (it has the index since session 42).

[x] **[High] Typed money amounts are misread:** *(session 41: `util/MoneyParser` now exists and is used for the employee salary; the product price/cost, discount and cash-received fields still need switching to it)*  "25.000" becomes 25 đ.** **NEW (3rd scan)** The product dialog (`AdminController`, price and cost, around lines 1292 and 1302) and the payment dialog *(DONE in code, session 42: every money box (product price and cost, discount, cash received) now uses `MoneyParser`: `25.000`, `25,000`, `25000đ` work, letters and exponents are refused with a clear message.)*
        (`OrderController.parseNonNegative`, around line 983) all call `new BigDecimal(text)`. The app shows money as `25.000đ` (dot as thousands separator), so a person who types it the same way saves a price of 25 đ
        (`Money.toDong` rounds `25.000` to 25). `25,000` is rejected, `1e5` is accepted, and `100.5` is silently rounded. A cashier typing a discount or cash received hits the same trap. Fix: one shared
        `MoneyParser` that drops `.`, `,`, spaces and a trailing `đ`/`d`, accepts digits only, and rejects the rest with a clear message; use it in all four fields and add a test. **(confirmed in code)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan** with `new BigDecimal(text)` + `Money.toDong`: `25.000` becomes 25, `1e5` becomes 100000, `100.5` becomes 101, `25,000` is rejected with NumberFormatException.
17. [x] **[High] Login by phone number is ambiguous and case-sensitive.** **NEW (3rd scan)** `UserDAO.findByUsername` runs `WHERE u.username = ? OR e.phone = ? OR c.phone = ? LIMIT 1` with no `ORDER BY`. *(DONE in code, session 42: login tries the username first (any capitals), then the phone in normalised form, in a fixed order; usernames that look like a phone are refused; staff phones are normalised and unique (`AccountService` + index `ux_employees_phone`); usernames are unique ignoring capitals (`ux_users_username_nocase`). Existing duplicates only produce a log warning and the index is skipped.)*
        Employee phones are not checked for uniqueness and are saved un-normalised (`phoneField.getText().trim()` in `AdminController`), usernames may be made only of digits (4-32 characters), and
        `existsByUsername` looks at usernames only. So: two staff with the same phone, or a username equal to someone's phone, makes it arbitrary which account a login opens; an employee saved as `+84901234567` cannot
        log in with `0901234567`. Usernames are also case-sensitive (`Nam` and `nam` can both exist) while `LoginThrottle` ignores case. Fix: try an exact username match first; refuse usernames that look like a phone number;
        normalise and uniqueness-check employee phones (same rule as customers); decide on case-insensitive usernames (`COLLATE NOCASE` unique index, after checking for duplicates). **(confirmed in code)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** two employees with the same phone were both saved with no error and a login by that phone returned the lower id; `Nam` and `nam` were both created while a search for `NAM` found neither; a 10-digit all-digit username is valid (`isValidUsername` = true).

Add a test. **(confirmed in code; needs an old-style row to trigger)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** `ImageStorage.deleteQuietly(<absolute path of a file in the temp folder>)` deleted that file (it was outside the images folder).

[x] **[Med] Errors on background threads and the UI thread never reach the log.** **NEW (5th scan)** No `setDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler` anywhere **(confirmed by search)**. `AppLogging` installs a file handler on the `java.util.logging` root logger, but an uncaught exception is printed to `System.err`, which on a *(DONE in code, session 42: `CrashHandler` logs every unhandled exception and shows one plain dialog (at most one per 10 s); `deleteProduct` also handles `DataAccessException`.)*
        packaged Windows app has no console. So a crash in a button handler or a timer leaves nothing in `logs/`, and the user sees nothing. Install a handler in `HelloApplication.init()` that logs the stack trace and shows one plain Vietnamese dialog (and combine with bug 2.5, the database-cannot-open case). **6th scan:** one concrete way to hit it: `AdminController.deleteProduct` calls `productDAO.delete(...)` on the UI thread with no try/catch, so any `DataAccessException` there (a locked database, say) is an uncaught exception.

[x] **[Med] The payment code trusts the totals it is given.** **NEW (5th scan)** `OrderDAO.payExistingOrder` stores the `discount`, `total` and each line's `unit_price`/`line_total` exactly as the screen passed them. It does not check `total = sum(lines) - discount`, that the discount is between 0 and the subtotal, that the items list is not empty, *(DONE in code, session 42: `OrderDAO.payExistingOrder` and the paid `insert` check lines, discount and total before writing and now write `subtotal`.)*
        and it never updates `orders.subtotal` (the column keeps whatever the last autosave wrote, so a paid order's subtotal can disagree with its lines). The payment dialog guards the discount, so nothing wrong happens today; it matters as soon as a second caller exists (feature 4.11, a service layer in 6.21, or a future import).
        Recompute the totals inside the transaction from the lines and reject a mismatch; write `subtotal` at payment; add tests for a negative discount, a discount larger than the subtotal and an empty order. **(confirmed by reading)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** `payExistingOrder` accepted discount `-5000` and total `1` for a 30,000đ line (stored exactly so, subtotal left at 30000), and it marked an order with no lines as PAID with total 0.

[x] **[Low] Search results can arrive out of order.** **NEW (6th scan)** `OrderHistoryWindow` starts a new `Async.run` (a new thread) on every keystroke in the search box and every filter change; each result replaces the table whenever it finishes. A slow earlier query finishing after a fast later one shows results for old text. It also calls `table.setDisable(true)` on every keystroke, so the table flickers and loses its selection while typing. Fix: a request counter (ignore a result that is not the latest) plus a 250 ms pause after typing; the same helper can serve the other screens. **(confirmed in code; the wrong-order case not reproduced, it needs a slow query)** *(DONE in code, session 42: history search waits 250 ms after typing, ignores out-of-date answers (request counter) and no longer disables the table.)*

If the admin answers "Không" to "close now" (`AdminController.handleRestoreData`), the till keeps selling and the restore is applied at whatever start comes next, perhaps days later: everything sold in between is replaced (it is only in `before-restore-*.zip`). Nothing on screen says a restore is waiting, there is no cancel, and the confirmation names only the file, not its date or what is in it. *(DONE in code, session 42: the admin home shows a banner with a "Hủy khôi phục" button while a restore is pending; at start-up a restore older than an hour is asked about once (No cancels it); the confirmation shows what the backup holds.)*

[x] **[Med] Restore is not all-or-nothing and skips some checks.** **NEW (7th scan)** (a) `replaceImages` deletes the whole `images/` folder **before** the database file is moved in; if the extraction or the move then fails (antivirus lock, full disk) the pictures are gone or partial while the database is still the old one. The comment "nothing is half-applied" is true only for the database. Extract the pictures into a temporary folder first, then swap the folders. **Second scan reproduced (a): a backup whose picture entries have a wrong CRC left 1 picture of 36 and still reported "current data kept"; `scheduleRestore` reads only the database entry, so it should read every entry to the end to check CRCs.** *(DONE in code, session 42: (a) pictures are extracted to a temporary folder and swapped in only when everything is ready, and put back on failure; every zip entry is read to its end when the backup is chosen, so a damaged picture is refused up front; (b) a backup or database with a newer `user_version` is refused; (c) temporary files are removed and only the newest 3 `restore-failed-*` are kept; (d) `qr-transfer.*` is restored.)*
        (b) `verifyDatabase` checks integrity and three tables, but **not `PRAGMA user_version`** (0 matches for `user_version` in `BackupService`): a backup made by a newer build is accepted, although item 4.23 promised that check; and `SchemaMigrations.upgrade` has no "this database is newer than the app" guard either (a restored or hand-copied newer database is opened with older code).
        (c) A failed restore leaves `data/lunavera.db.restoring` behind, and `restore-failed-*` files pile up in `data/`; clean them up. (d) `qr-transfer.*` is written into every backup zip (`writeZip`) but **never restored** (only the database and `images/` are), so after a restore on a new machine the bank QR is missing although the backup holds it.
        Tests: pictures survive a failed restore; a newer `user_version` is refused with a clear message; the transfer QR comes back. **(confirmed in code; not run)**

71. [x] **[Low] `DatabaseConfig.getConnection` leaks the connection when the set-up after connecting fails.** **NEW (11th scan)** On SQLite it opens the connection, then runs `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, `busy_timeout`, `synchronous` and registers the `VN_KEY` function; none of that is inside a `try` that closes the connection. If one of those calls throws (the file is locked longer than the timeout, a disk I/O error, a damaged file), the exception reaches the caller but the open connection (and its file handle, and in WAL mode its share of the `-shm` file) stays until the garbage collector finds it. On a shop PC that retries every 30 s (`SessionGuard`), a long fault piles up open handles, and on Windows an open handle blocks a backup restore or a file delete. **(confirmed in code)** Wrap the set-up in `try { ... } catch (SQLException | RuntimeException e) { connection.close(); throw e; }`; test with a connection source that fails on the pragma. *(DONE in code, Session 87: the set-up moved into `DatabaseConfig.configureSqlite`, which closes the connection when any step throws. Test: `DatabaseConfigConnectionTest` with a stand-in connection; run here: passed.)*

The dump command now passes `--no-tablespaces` (MySQL 8.0.21+ otherwise needs the PROCESS privilege that a database-level user lacks); if the client is too old to know the option and says so (\"unknown option\", \"unrecognized option\" or \"unknown variable\" naming `no-tablespaces`) the same dump is run again without it; any other failure is not retried and removes the half file. Tested here with a stand-in `mysqldump` shell script (3 end-to-end tests: retried without the option; known option run once; another failure reported and no file left) plus 2 text tests: `MariaDbBackupTest` 7 pass (the process tests need a Unix shell). `docs/DATABASE.md` has a new troubleshooting row. NOT run against a real MySQL 8 server: a person with one should press the backup once (section 0). Not touched: the 180-second limit that never applies (2.76).)*

[x] **[Low] The category dialog changes the table's own row before the save has worked, and a refused save leaves the refused text on screen.** **NEW (14th scan)** `CategoryDialog.show` takes `existing` (the very `Category` object the table shows) and calls `setName`, `setDescription` and `setActive` on it first, then `CATEGORIES.update(category)`. If the database refuses (the name is taken, the connection dropped) the dialog stays open, which is right, but when the person then presses Cancel nothing reloads (`afterSave` runs only after a save), so the row keeps the refused name until the next reload, the next edit opens with it, and the table says something the database does not. The product dialog does the same on `existing` but always calls `reloadProducts()` afterwards, so only the category screen shows the problem. **(confirmed in code)** Edit a copy (`new Category(existing)`) and copy the values back only after a successful save, or reload in every case. *(DONE in code, Session 87: the dialog edits a copy and copies the values to the table's `Category` only after the database accepted the save. Not tried on a screen; there is no test for a JavaFX dialog.)*
77. [x] **[Med] A decimal amount typed in a money box is read as a number 10 or 100 times too big.** **NEW (15th scan)** `MoneyParser.parseDong` (2.16) drops every `.`, `,` and space wherever it sits, so it cannot tell a thousands separator from a decimal point. **Reproduced on this tree:** `25.5` and `25,5` give 255, `10.50` gives 1050, `1,234.5` gives 12345, `25,000.50` gives 2500050, `1.000,5` gives 10005, `1,2,3` gives 123. `parseDong` and `parseOptionalDong` serve the product price and cost, the discount, the cash received, the salary and the CSV import, so a price typed `25.5` (for 25.500) or a pasted `10.50` is saved without a word, 10 to 100 times too small or too large. 3.46 describes the same flaw only for the CSV stock column. Fix: accept a separator only when exactly three digits follow it (`25.000`, `1,250,000`); anything else with a `.` or `,` is refused with the existing message plus "không nhập số thập phân". Add the cases above to `MoneyParserTest`. *(14th pass: DONE. `MoneyParser` now accepts `.`, `,` and a space only as a thousands separator: one kind throughout, a first group of 1 to 3 digits not starting with 0, every later group exactly 3 digits. `25.000`, `1,250,000`, `8 000 000` (also with non-breaking spaces) and `50.000đ` are read; `25.5`, `25,5`, `10.50`, `1,234.5`, `25,000.50`, `1.000,5`, `1,2,3`, `1250.000`, `0.500` and mixed separators are refused with \"Số tiền chỉ được gồm chữ số (ví dụ 8000000 hoặc 8.000.000); không nhập số thập phân.\" (the optional form used by the discount and cash boxes returns null for them, which the screen already reports). 4 new `MoneyParserTest` cases, run here with the old ones and `ProductCsvTest`, `MoneyGuardTest`: 19 pass. Effect on the CSV import: a price cell written like `10.50` is now a row error instead of 1050. Not changed: `k`/`tr` suffixes (5.33).)*
78. [x] **[Med] When the disk is full, every sale fails with a message that says "try again".** **NEW (15th scan)** **Reproduced** on a 14 MB filesystem filled to the brim: 400 of 400 sales failed (nothing half-saved: no order, stock and points unchanged), with the cause `SQLITE_IOERR_SHMSIZE`. `DataAccessException.hintFor` knows a wrong password, a lost server and a busy database, but not a full disk, so the cashier sees only the generic "Đã xảy ra lỗi. Hãy thử lại; nếu lỗi vẫn còn, báo người phụ trách" and retries again and again. A full disk is handled at start-up (`StartupProblem`) and in the backup (free-space check), nowhere else. Fix: map `SQLITE_FULL`, `SQLITE_IOERR*` and MariaDB 1021/1114 to "Ổ đĩa đầy hoặc không ghi được. Hãy giải phóng dung lượng rồi thử lại; đơn vẫn được giữ trên màn hình"; see 4.24 for a warning before it happens (free space under 200 MB on the data drive). *(14th pass: DONE. `DataAccessException.hintFor` now recognises a full or unwritable disk and says \"Ổ đĩa đầy hoặc không ghi được, nên chưa lưu được. Hãy giải phóng dung lượng trên ổ đĩa chứa thư mục dữ liệu rồi thử lại.\" instead of the generic \"try again\": SQLite by result code (`SQLITE_FULL` or any `SQLITE_IOERR`, extended codes included, read from `SQLiteException.getResultCode().code & 0xff`), MariaDB by error 1021, 1114 or \"Got error 28\", and the operating system's own words as a fallback, anywhere in the cause chain. New `DiskFullHintTest` (6) beside the existing `DataAccessExceptionTest` (5), run here: 11 pass. NOT checked against a real full disk.)*
79. [x] **[Med] While a payment is being saved, the POS screen stays fully usable, and the result can wipe or mix up what the cashier did meanwhile.** **NEW (15th scan)** `OrderController.completeOrder` disables only `paymentButton` and runs the payment with `Async.runOrdered`. The tables, dish cards, +/−, customer boxes and the sidebar stay live (`orderInProgress` says an order exists, not that one is being paid). If the cashier taps another table or a dish in that moment (a slow MariaDB link makes it seconds), the success callback runs `cart.clear()`, `resetOrderAfterPayment()` and sets `activeOpenOrder = null` on whatever is on screen then, so the other table's lines vanish from the screen while its order still exists in the database, and the next autosave tries to create a second OPEN order for that table (refused by 3.22, shown as an error). **(confirmed in code that nothing blocks the screen; not reproduced, JavaFX is not available here)** Fix: a `paymentInFlight` flag that disables the table map, dish list, cart buttons and sidebar (or an overlay "Đang lưu…") until the callback ends, and have the callback check that `selectedTableNumber` is still the table that was paid. Related: 2.55, 2.65, 2.75, 5.16. *(14th pass: DONE in code. A `paymentInFlight` flag, set from the moment the payment is confirmed until the save finishes (and cleared first thing in both callbacks), disables the whole scene and shows the wait cursor, and the table, product, cart, start/edit-order and payment handlers return at once while it is set, which also covers keyboard shortcuts. Together with the reorder of 2.65 a callback can no longer wipe something the cashier did meanwhile. NOT seen on screen; on a slow MariaDB link the grey-out should be visible for a moment.)*

80. [x] **[Low] A price typed with the real dong sign "₫" is refused.** **NEW (16th scan)** `MoneyParser.parseDong` strips only the endings `vnđ`, `vnd`, `đ` and `d` (the letter), and every other character is an error. The app itself prints the letter ("12.000đ", `CurrencyUtil`), so copying a price from the app works, but the sign `₫` (U+20AB) that Windows, phone keyboards, bank apps and supplier price lists use is not accepted. **Reproduced by running the class:** `10.000đ` and `10 000₫` are read, `10000₫` and `₫10000` give "Số tiền chỉ được gồm chữ số". It fails in every money box that uses the parser (price, cost, discount, cash received, salary, CSV). **(confirmed by running the code)** Strip a leading or trailing `₫` and the other ending as well, case-insensitively and with `Locale.ROOT` (the parser calls `toLowerCase()` with the computer's locale), and add the cases to `MoneyParserTest`. Related: 2.77. *(DONE in code, Session 87: `MoneyParser` accepts the dong sign U+20AB first or last, and the lower-casing uses `Locale.ROOT`. Tests: `theRealDongSignIsAccepted`, run here: passed.)*

81. [x] **[Low] The product CSV import accepts three kinds of questionable row without a word.** **NEW (16th scan)** Reproduced by running `ProductCsv.parse` on sample files: (1) in the "Đang bán" column anything except `không`, `0`, `false` and `no` means "on sale" (`a.equals("khong") || ...` then `setActive(!...)`), so `Ngừng`, `Hết`, `Tạm ngưng`, `n`, `off` or a typo put the product on sale; (2) a cost above the price (`45000` and `90000`) is imported silently, while the product form asks "vẫn lưu?" for the same input (3.19); (3) a row with more cells than the header, or fewer, is read as far as it goes and the rest is ignored. **(confirmed by running the code)** Accept only an explicit list for the active column (`có`, `co`, `1`, `true`, `yes`, `x` and `không`, `0`, `false`, `no`, `ngừng bán`, `ngung ban`); anything else becomes a problem line; show cost-above-price rows as a warning list before the confirmation (the file is still importable); count extra cells as a notice. Tests with the rows above. Related: 2.69, 3.46. *(DONE in code, Session 87: the "Đang bán" column accepts only `có, 1, true, yes, x, đang bán` and `không, 0, false, no, ngừng bán` (empty or no column still means on sale); anything else is a problem line. A cost above the price and a row with more or fewer cells than the header are imported but listed as warnings in the confirmation. Tests: `theOnSaleColumnOnlyAcceptsAnExplicitWord`, `aCostAboveThePriceAndRaggedRowsAreImportedWithAWarning`; run here: passed.)*

82. [x] **[Med] A database password with a backslash, a space at either end, or `\u` in it is silently changed, and a password ending in a backslash swallows the next setting.** **NEW (19th scan)** `DatabaseSettings.load` reads `data/database.properties` with `Properties.load` and then `.trim()`s every value. Run on the real class: `db.password=ab\cd` is used as `abcd`; `ab\\cd` as `ab\cd`; `x\u0041y` as `xAy`; `  lead` and `trail   ` lose their spaces; and `abc\` (a backslash at the end of the line) joins the next line, so the password becomes `abcdb.type=sqlite` and the `db.type=sqlite` setting under it is lost, which sends a SQLite shop to MariaDB. Characters like `#`, `!`, `=` and `:` are fine. The shop sees only "Access denied" or "cannot connect", with the password written correctly in the file. A password made by a generator, as `docs/DATABASE.md` advises ("use a long one"), can easily contain a backslash. **(reproduced by running `DatabaseSettings.fromProperties`)** Fix: read the file in a way that has no escapes (read the lines yourself, split at the first `=`, and trim only a trailing line break), or at least refuse a value that contains `\` with a message, and write in `docs/DATABASE.md` and `database.properties.example` that the password must not contain a backslash; add a test with the nine cases above. *(14th pass: DONE. `database.properties` is read literally: `DatabaseSettings.parseLiteral` splits each line at the first `=` and keeps the value exactly (a backslash is an ordinary character, `\\u0041` is not decoded, spaces at either end of the password are kept, a trailing backslash no longer swallows the next setting, so a SQLite shop is not sent to MariaDB); comment lines start with `#` or `!`; a Notepad byte-order mark and Windows line ends are handled; a password of only spaces still means \"no password\" (XAMPP). `-Dlunavera.db.password` is used as typed too. `DatabasePropertiesFileTest` (10), run here with `DatabaseSettingsTest`: 24 pass. Docs: `docs/DATABASE.md` and `database.properties.example` now say write paths with single backslashes (older files with doubled ones still work on Windows) and no comment after a value. **Behaviour change to know:** a value in the file is no longer trimmed for the password only; every other setting is still trimmed by `fromProperties`.)*

83. [ ] **[Low] A printer driver that throws leaves the print job open, and printing blocks the screen.** **NEW (22nd scan)** Item 2.14 handled a `printPage` that returns false. In `HoaDonController.handlePrint` the call sits in `try { printed = job.printPage(toPrint); } finally { remove the transform }` with no `catch`: a `RuntimeException` from the driver (a printer that went offline, a network roll printer that times out) skips `endJob`/`cancelJob`, so the job and its queue entry stay open and the cashier gets no message except the generic crash dialog. The whole call, including `showPrintDialog`, also runs on the screen thread, so a slow network printer freezes the till until it answers. The method also reads `job.getJobSettings().getPageLayout()` twice in a row (a leftover line). **(confirmed in code; a faulty printer is needed to see it)** Catch `RuntimeException` around `printPage`, call `cancelJob()` and show "Không thể in" with the reason; print on a background thread (printing may be started off the FX thread) and re-enable the button afterwards. *(PART DONE, Session 87, left open: `printPage` is now inside a `catch (RuntimeException)` that cancels the job and shows "Không thể in" with a short reason; the duplicate `getPageLayout` line is gone. Not done: printing on a background thread. JavaFX requires a node that is on screen to be printed on the screen thread, and the receipt is on screen, so a slow network printer can still freeze the till for a moment. Not tried with a real printer.)*

84. [x] **[Med] Opening an old customer-role account in the account dialog and pressing "Lưu" turns it into a staff account.** **NEW (24th scan)** `AccountController.openAccountDialog` fills the role box with `existing.getRole()` only when it is not `CUSTOMER`; otherwise it shows **Nhân viên**, and the box offers only Admin and Employee. The save then calls `accountService.update`, which writes `form.role()` through `changeRoleAndStatus`. The account list still shows these accounts (the role filter has "Khách hàng"; item 2A.23 stopped them logging in but they are still in the table), so an administrator who opens one to fix a typo or to lock it and presses Save has given that customer login a staff role, and a later login with it is a cashier's. The dialog never says the role changed, and the security log line says only "role CUSTOMER -> EMPLOYEE". **(confirmed in code; the screen was not run)** Keep the role unchanged when the account is a `CUSTOMER` one (show it read-only, or hide the box), and make `AccountService.update` refuse a change of role away from `CUSTOMER` unless an explicit action asks for it; add the case to `AccountServiceTest`. *(14th pass: DONE in code. `AccountService.update` refuses to change an old customer-role account into any other role (\"Đây là tài khoản khách hàng cũ, không thể chuyển thành tài khoản nhân viên...\"); keeping the role (to fix or lock the account) works. The account dialog shows such an account with its role fixed to the customer role and the box disabled, so Save sends the same role. 3 new `AccountServiceTest` cases (refused as employee and as administrator with the role unchanged in the database; locking through the form keeps the role; a staff account can still switch between employee and administrator), compiled, NOT run.)*

85. [x] **[Med] After a crash, a restore's safety copy (and the restore itself) can lose the last sales that were still in the write-ahead log.** **NEW (25th scan)** The database runs in WAL mode (6.16): committed sales sit in `lunavera.db-wal` until a checkpoint moves them into `lunavera.db`, and a power cut or a killed process leaves the `-wal` file behind. A restore that was parked before the crash is applied in `LunaveraApplication.init()` *before* the database is opened, so nothing has recovered the log yet: `BackupService.applyPendingRestore` writes the "before-restore" safety zip with `writeZip(safety, database, ...)`, which copies the bare `lunavera.db` file only (the `-wal` is not included), and then deletes `-journal`, `-wal` and `-shm` before moving the restored file in. Everything sold since the last checkpoint is therefore missing from the safety copy and is gone from the shop's disk, although the message promises "Dữ liệu trước đó được lưu tại ...". **(confirmed in code; the crash itself was not staged)** Before the safety copy, open the live file once (SQLite replays the log on the first connection) and take it with `VACUUM INTO` (the way `DatabaseConfig.backupTo` does) or run `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` and close; only then delete the side files. Test: write a row, copy the db and `-wal` pair to a folder without checkpointing, run the restore, and look for the row inside the safety zip. *(14th pass: DONE in code. `BackupService.applyPendingRestore` now opens the live file once (SQLite replays the leftover write-ahead log) and runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` BEFORE the safety zip is written and before the side files are deleted (`foldLeftoverLogInto`); if the file cannot be opened the `-wal` file is kept in `data/backups/` as `lunavera.db-wal.unfolded-<time>` instead of being deleted. Checked the mechanism on real SQLite (Python): a sale only in the log is missing (0) from a plain copy of the main file and present (1) after the fold. 3 new `BackupServiceTest` cases (a staged crash with a sale only in the log ends up in the safety copy and the restored database is the backup; folding with no log is a no-op; an unfoldable log is kept) compiled, NOT run (need the SQLite driver).)*

## 2A. Security flaws  **NEW (4th scan)**
This is a local desktop app with one till, so the main risks are a curious or careless person at the till, a lost computer or USB stick, and mistakes that lock the shop out. Related items found earlier:
bug 2.2 (default admin password stays usable), bug 2.3 (unlimited discounts), bug 2.12 (password hash strength), edge case 3.8 (any employee can empty a table) and feature 4.2 (audit log).

1. [x] **[Med] CSV export can run a formula in Excel.** `ReportWindow.csv()` only quotes commas, quotes and line breaks. A product named `=HYPERLINK("http://...")` or starting with `+`, `-` or `@` becomes a live formula when the report is opened in Excel. **DONE in code, session 43:** CSV cells that start with `= + - @`, a tab or a carriage return get a leading apostrophe (`Csv.cell`; used by the report export; tested).
       Prefix such cells with an apostrophe or a space (and treat a leading tab or carriage return the same way). Add a test. **(confirmed in code)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** `csv()` returned `=HYPERLINK(...)`, `+1+1`, `@SUM(A1)` and `-2+3` unchanged (only the first got quotes, from its inner double quotes); a value with a bare carriage return (`a\rb`) is not quoted either.
2. [x] **[Med] The "last admin" rule can be beaten.** The check (`countActiveAdmins() <= 1`) lives in the screen, then `UserDAO.updateStatus` / `updateRole` run as a separate step with no guard. Two admins working at once can lock or demote each other and leave no active admin, *(DONE in code, session 42: the rule is one UPDATE inside `AccountService`, checked by the database in the same statement; tested with the last admin.)*
       and the only way back in is editing the database by hand. Enforce it in the DAO in one transaction (for example `UPDATE ... WHERE (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users WHERE role='ADMIN' AND status='ACTIVE') > 1`) and test it. **(confirmed in code)**
3. [x] **[Med] Permissions are checked only in the screens.** `requireAdmin()` and `Session.isAdmin()` sit in controllers; `OrderDAO.cancelPaidOrder`, `UserDAO.updateRole`, `updatePasswordHash`, `ProductDAO.update` and the backup code check nothing. One forgotten check in a future handler exposes the action. **First step, session 43 (finished in session 79, see the DONE note below):** creating, editing and locking accounts, restoring a backup and refunding a paid order now refuse a non-admin inside the service/DAO (`Permissions.requireAdmin`; tested with a cashier and with nobody signed in). Two questions were still open then; both are closed further down (employees and the accounts list: 13th pass; the checks inside the DAOs: session 79). *(8th pass, by reading the callers: account creation, editing and locking, restore and refund are enforced below the screen already. `UserDAO.updateStatus` and `updateRole` have no caller in the app at all (the account service writes its own SQL); `updatePasswordHash` is called only by the person's own password change and the login re-hash. Product and category writes are called only from `ProductController`, `CategoryController` and `CategoryDialog` (one `AdminController` before the 13th-pass split), which send a non-admin away in `initialize()` through `AdminScreen.blockNonAdmin`, and since the 13th pass the delete flows also check the permission in `CategoryService` and `ProductService` (so the old question about employees seeing the accounts list is closed: they cannot open it). That last part, putting `Permissions.requireAdmin` into `ProductDAO`/`CategoryDAO`, was done in session 79 (see the DONE note below).)*
       *(Original advice, kept for history; both parts are settled: the permission check now sits in the service/DAO layer, and employees cannot open the accounts list at all, see the 13th-pass note above.)* **(confirmed)**
       **DONE in code (session 79, by reading and by running the real classes; no database here):** `ProductDAO.insert/update/delete/setActive` and `CategoryDAO.insert/update/deleteIfUnused` now call `Permissions.requireAdmin` first, so a cashier, or nobody, is refused below the screen as with accounts, restore and refund. Run on the real classes: nobody and a cashier are refused on all seven methods, an administrator passes the check. The reads (`findAll`, `findLowStock`, ...) stay open because the sales screen needs them as a cashier. The six test classes that wrote products or categories without signing in (`CategoryDAOTest`, `EdgeCasesTest`, `ProductDAOTest`, `ProductNameCheckTest`, `UniqueViolationTest`, `TextNormalizerTest`) now sign an administrator in (`TestSession.admin()`) and out again; `PermissionsTest` has three new tests (cashier refused on the seven writes, nobody refused, a cashier can still read the menu). Raw SQL writers (`MenuSeeder`, the importer) never went through these DAOs, so start-up seeding is unaffected. The question about employees seeing the accounts list was closed in the 13th pass. **Not run here:** the DAO tests need SQLite, so run `./mvnw clean test` once; if a test that writes products fails with "chỉ dành cho Quản trị viên", it is missing `TestSession.admin()`.
       **Checked again 2026-10-24 against the merged tree (nothing open is left in this item):** `Permissions.requireAdmin` is called inside `ProductDAO` (insert, import, update, delete, setActive), `CategoryDAO` (insert, update, delete), `UserDAO.updateStatus` and `updateRole` (added when the fourth branch was merged in Session 93, so the 8th-pass remark that they "have no caller" no longer matters: a caller is refused anyway; `UserDaoPermissionTest`), `OrderDAO` (refund of a paid order, order export), `AccountService` (create, edit, lock), `BackupService` (both restore entry points) and `CategoryService`/`ProductService` (deletes). `PermissionsTest` (6 tests) and `UserDaoPermissionTest` (2) pin it down. `updatePasswordHash` stays open on purpose: it is the person's own password change and the login re-hash. Not run: Maven.
4. [ ] **[Med] Customer data and password hashes are stored and backed up in plain form.** The database holds customer names and phone numbers and every PBKDF2 hash, and the backup button copies all of it to any folder or USB stick with no warning. **PARTLY DONE, session 43:** PARTLY: the backup-done message now warns that the zip holds customer names, phones and password hashes unencrypted. Not done at that time: a passphrase-protected backup, owner-only folder permissions, and the legal notice (ask an adviser). **Checked 2026-10-23:** the passphrase-protected backup is in the code (`BackupCrypto`, `BackupPassphraseDialog`, session 79) and the owner-only folder is set on Windows too (2A.14, session 84); only the legal notice is still open, so this item stays open. *(8th pass: on Linux and macOS the data folder is now set to owner-only (`rwx------`) at every start, `DataPaths.restrictToOwner`, tested. Windows has no such call in plain Java and nothing was changed there. The passphrase-protected backup is not done.)*
       Add a warning in the backup dialog, offer a passphrase-protected backup, keep the live database readable only by the shop's Windows user, and note that moving data into the project folder (1.3) makes the exposure larger.
       Customers are never told their phone number is stored and cannot ask for removal (feature 4.8); Vietnam has a personal-data decree, so ask an adviser what notice or consent the shop needs. **(confirmed for the storage; the legal part is for an adviser)**
       **More done in session 79 (code and text; the backup part was run here, the dialog was not):** (1) **Passphrase-protected backup.** New `BackupCrypto` (AES-256-GCM in 64 KiB blocks, key from PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with 600,000 rounds, random salt and IV, header and a last-block flag authenticated so a changed header, a reordered or a cut-short file is refused) and `BackupService.createEncryptedBackup` / `inspect(path, passphrase)` / `scheduleRestore(path, passphrase)` (a `.lvbak` file; the plain zip exists only for a moment in the program's temp folder and is always deleted). The dashboard asks "Sao lưu dữ liệu" for an optional passphrase (both boxes empty = a plain zip as before) and "Khôi phục dữ liệu" asks for it when the chosen file is locked (`BackupPassphraseDialog`). `BackupCryptoTest` (15 tests) was **run here against the real class: all pass** (round trips of 0 bytes, 1 KB, exactly 2 blocks and 3 blocks and a bit, wrong or empty passphrase, a changed header byte, a changed byte in a later block, a file cut mid-block and at a block boundary, a header asking for 2 billion rounds, the plain text not visible in the locked file). `BackupServiceTest` has 4 new tests and `BackupPassphraseDialogTest` 2; they need SQLite / JavaFX and were only syntax-checked here. Only for SQLite shops (the in-app restore is what opens the file again); a MariaDB shop restores by hand, so its zip stays plain: keep it on a BitLocker drive or lock it with 7-Zip. The automatic daily backups stay unlocked (the program cannot ask at night). (2) **Windows folder rights:** still not set by the program (plain Java cannot do it safely, and a wrong ACL could lock the shop out of its own data), but the README now explains the risk of a folder directly under `C:\` and gives the one `icacls` command that keeps only the user, the system and the administrators (by account id, so it works on any Windows language). **Still open:** the legal notice to customers (an adviser decides), enforcing the folder rights from the program, and a locked automatic backup.

The wrapper refuses any other download. The enforcer rule (`[3.8.5,)`) and every plugin version in the pom are explicit, so the Maven change should not change the build; the pom comments say 3.9.16. **First `./mvnw` run needs the network and downloads about 9 MB.** (2) **Dependency check in CI:** new `.github/workflows/dependency-scan.yml` runs Google's OSV-Scanner (reusable workflow pinned to the commit of release v2.7.0) against `pom.xml` on every pom change and every Monday, and fails on a known vulnerability (upload to code scanning is off). It was not run (GitHub only). (3) Dependabot (already there) keeps the pins current. **Still open:** the wrapper jar in the repository is Maven Wrapper 3.1.0 (its properties still name 3.3.2, which is only used when the jar is missing): replace it and add `wrapperSha256Sum` with `./mvnw wrapper:wrapper -Dmaven=3.9.16` on a machine that reaches Maven Central; pinning `ci.yml`'s two actions to commits (6.51; done since, see 6.51); signing the installer once one exists.
