[x] **[Med] The security events share one small rotating log, and a flood of failed logins can push the history out.** **NEW (8th scan)** `SecurityLog` writes through `java.util.logging` into the same `data/logs/app-%u-%g.log` as everything else, and `AppLogging` keeps only 3 files of 1 MB. Every wrong password and every attempt made while the delay is running writes a WARNING line (`AuthenticationService.login`), so holding Enter on the login screen fills the 3 MB in a short time and the earlier "discount refused", "account locked", "password reset" lines are overwritten. **(confirmed in code: file count/size and one line per attempt)** Fix: a separate `security.log` handler (bigger, one file per month, nothing else in it), and at most one "blocked" line per username per minute. *(DONE in code, session 83: a new handler, `SecurityLogFile`, on the `lunavera.security` logger writes `data/logs/security-YYYY-MM.log`: one file per month, nothing else writes to it, a month's file that reaches 5 MB continues in `security-YYYY-MM.1.log` and so on up to 20 parts (a flood can never fill the disk), months older than 24 are deleted at start, one line per record (`2026-10-18 14:03:11 WARNING  ...`), line breaks inside a message become spaces. `AppLogging.install()` starts it in a step of its own, so a problem with the normal log file leaves the security events with theirs; the lines still also go to the normal log. `LogRateLimiter` and `SecurityLog.warnLimited(key, text)`: one line per kind per user name per minute and at most 30 lines a minute in all (typing a different name each time cannot flood it either); the next line says `(+N similar not written)`. Used for the four login lines of `AuthenticationService` (blocked, no such user, wrong password, locked account), for refused actions (`Permissions.requireAdmin`) and for the "transfer QR picture CHANGED" warning, which was written on every check. Tests: `LogRateLimiterTest` (10), `SecurityLogFileTest` (9), `SecurityLogTest` (6), `AuthenticationServiceTest.aFloodOfWrongPasswordsLeavesAFewSecurityLogLinesNotOnePerAttempt` (300 attempts leave 2 lines), `AppLoggingTest.securityEventsAlsoGoToAMonthlyFileThatHoldsNothingElse`. All of them were compiled and run here with stand-in libraries (no Maven): 62 + 3 passed; the wiring was also run end to end. Not done: a screen that shows the file (4.44, still open), and the file is not part of the backup (logs never are). Owner-only folder permissions are done since (2A.14).)* *(Checked 2026-10-24: the wiring is `AppLogging.install()` -> `SecurityLog.install(logsDir)` -> `SecurityLogFile`; `warnLimited` is used by the five login lines of `AuthenticationService` and by `Permissions.requireAdmin`; the two "not done" points above are still true and are tracked elsewhere (4.44 is open, backups do not include `logs/`). Nothing else is left open.)*

26. [x] **[Med] A MariaDB shop with a second till sends everything over the network unencrypted, and a hung server freezes the till.** **NEW (8th scan)** `DatabaseSettings.jdbcUrl` builds `jdbc:mariadb://host:port/db?connectTimeout=5000` and nothing else: there is no way to ask for TLS (`sslMode`) and no `socketTimeout`. `docs/DATABASE.md` section 7 allows other tills to connect, and then customer names, phone numbers, password hashes and the database password itself cross the shop's wifi in clear. Without a read timeout, a server that stops answering (power cut at the router, a stuck query) blocks the call forever, and many calls run on the screen thread (see 6.14), so the window freezes with no message. **(confirmed in code)** Fix: optional `db.ssl=true` / `db.options=` in `database.properties`, `socketTimeout` of about 30 s, and say in the docs that a remote server needs TLS. *(DONE in code, session 83: `DatabaseSettings` has new keys. `db.ssl=true` means TLS with the server's certificate and name checked (`sslMode=verify-full`); `db.sslMode=` (disable, trust, verify-ca or verify-full) chooses exactly and wins over `db.ssl`, and `db.ssl=true` with `db.sslMode=disable` is refused; `db.sslServerCert=` is the certificate or CA file of a self-signed server (it must exist when the mode checks certificates, a name without a folder is read from the data folder, it goes into the URL as `serverSslCert=` with forward slashes and the characters `space % & = # ?` escaped); `db.socketTimeoutMs=` (default 30000, 0 = wait for ever, otherwise 1000 to 3,600,000). The URL is now `jdbc:mariadb://host:port/db?connectTimeout=5000&socketTimeout=30000&sslMode=disable` (or the chosen mode). A server on another computer with nothing encrypting the connection gets a WARNING in the log at start-up (`insecureRemoteWarning`, no password in it) and the line on the login screen says "KHÔNG mã hóa" ("mã hóa TLS" when it is encrypted, "chưa kiểm tra chứng chỉ" for `trust`); a server on this computer says nothing. An IPv6 host is now put in square brackets in the URL. SQLite ignores all of it. Docs: `docs/DATABASE.md` sections 3 and 7 (server side, `REQUIRE SSL`, which mode to pick, the timeout) and `database.properties.example`. Tests: 13 new in `DatabaseSettingsTest`, 2 in `LocationSummaryTest`; compiled and run here with stand-in libraries. Not tried: a real MariaDB with TLS (section 0); the URL parameter names `sslMode`, `serverSslCert` and `socketTimeout` are those of MariaDB Connector/J 3.x as documented, the pom has 3.4.1. Not covered: the `mysqldump` backup, which does not read these keys (2A.42).)*

27. [x] **[Low] Anyone at the till can keep a known account ("admin") on the login delay.** **NEW (8th scan)** `LoginThrottle` is per user name and the name is the only key, so a customer or a former employee who types "admin" and a wrong password every 30 seconds keeps the real admin waiting up to 30 s at every try; the delay never needs the attacker to succeed. **(reasoned from `LoginThrottle`, not tried)** The delay is short and nobody is locked out, so this is only a nuisance, but it is worth knowing before 2A.19 stores the counters in a table: do not make the delay longer or permanent without an admin way out.

28. [x] **[Med] The login delay can be skipped by typing the same staff phone number in another spelling.** **NEW (9th scan)** `UserDAO.findByUsername` lets a person sign in with the user name **or** the staff phone, and it normalises the phone first (`+84 901 111 222`, `84901111222`, `0901.111.222` and `0901111222` all find the same account). `LoginThrottle.key` only trims and lower-cases the typed text, and `AuthenticationService.login` asks the throttle with the text as typed. So one account has one throttle bucket for its user name and a separate fresh bucket (2 free tries, then the delay) for every spelling of its phone: dozens of buckets, and the 30-second ceiling never bites. A staff phone number is much easier to learn than a password. **(confirmed in code; not tried against a running app)** Fix: throttle on the account that was found (its id) as well as on the typed text, or normalise the text with `ValidationUtil.normalizePhone` before it becomes the key; add a test that five spellings of one phone share one counter. *(DONE in code, Session 83: `LoginThrottle.key` now reduces a phone to one form with `ValidationUtil.normalizePhone` and drops control characters, so every spelling of a staff phone shares one counter; and the delay is also kept per account (`secondsUntilAllowedForAccount`, `recordFailureForAccount`, `recordSuccessForAccount`, a bucket name no typed text can equal), checked in `AuthenticationService.login` once the account is found and before the password is tried, so the user name and every spelling of the phone share one wait and one reset on success. Tests: three in `LoginThrottleTest`, three in `AuthenticationServiceTest`; run here with a stand-in for the database classes: 36 passed.)*

29. [x] **[Low] A restore accepts a backup that has no active administrator.** **NEW (9th scan)** `BackupService.verifyDatabase` checks `integrity_check`, the schema version and that the tables `users`, `products` and `orders` exist, nothing about the accounts. A backup made before the "last administrator" rule (2A.2) existed, or one whose admins were all locked, restores without error; after the restart nobody can sign in as admin, and the first-start dialog does not help because accounts exist. **(confirmed what is checked; the situation is rare)** Fix: in `verifyDatabase` also require `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users WHERE role='ADMIN' AND status='ACTIVE'` to be at least 1, and say so in the restore dialog (5.22 already shows what a backup holds). *(DONE in code, Session 85: `BackupService.verifyDatabase` also requires at least one account with role ADMIN and status ACTIVE and refuses the backup with a message that says so; it applies to the preview, to scheduling and to the restore itself. Test: `aBackupWithoutAnActiveAdministratorIsRefused` (needs SQLite, not run here).)*

30. [x] **[Low] Looking at a backup before restoring it reads the zip without the size limits the restore uses.** **NEW (10th scan)** `BackupService.inspectInto` copies the `lunavera.db` entry with a plain `Files.copy(zip, databaseCopy, ...)` and reads the info entry with `zip.readAllBytes()`, while the restore itself (item 2A.21) uses `copyCapped(..., MAX_DATABASE_BYTES)` and `copyCapped(..., MAX_PICTURE_BYTES)`. So a damaged or deliberately made zip (or the wrong big file picked by mistake) can fill the temporary disk or the memory during the preview, before any limit applies. **(confirmed in code; not tried with a real oversized zip)** Use `copyCapped` for the database entry, cap the info entry at a few KB, and add a test with an entry larger than the cap (the cap can be a parameter so the test stays small). *(DONE in code, Session 85, together with 2A.40: see there.)*

31. [x] **[Low] Typing 1,000 different user names wipes the login delay of every account, "admin" included.** **NEW (10th scan)** `LoginThrottle.recordFailure` does `states.clear()` when `MAX_TRACKED` (1000) names are tracked, and the comment says an attacker "only resets everyone's counters". Each wrong attempt costs the attacker one password hash (a fraction of a second), so a script or a keyboard-emulating USB stick needs only minutes to reset the delay and then get two free tries again. It is a speed bump rather than a lock, so this is Low, but the cap that protects memory should not also erase the protection. **(confirmed in code)** When the map is full, drop the expired entries first, then the oldest ones, never all; test with 1,001 names that the entry for "admin" survives. Related: 2A.19, 2A.27, 2A.28. *(DONE in code, session 84: `LoginThrottle.recordFailure` no longer clears the table. When a NEW name arrives and 1,000 are tracked, `makeRoom` first drops every entry that has been quiet for 15 minutes (it would be forgotten anyway), and if that frees nothing it drops the weakest one: a name that is not waiting before one that is, then fewer wrong attempts, then the oldest. Names typed in a flood have one attempt and no wait, so they push each other out and not the name that has a delay running; a name already in the table is never pushed out by its own next failure (the old code cleared everything even then). Tests in `LoginThrottleTest`: 3,000 other names leave admin's delay untouched, a full table loses exactly one entry, a name in a full table keeps counting, fewer-failures-first, everyone-waiting, quiet-first. What remains: a flood can still cost a name that has only its two free attempts (it falls back to 0 of 2), which gives an attacker at most two extra tries per name per flood; the delay is still kept in memory only (2A.19).)*

32. [x] **[Low] The first login with a name that does not exist is slower than the next ones.** **NEW (10th scan)** `AuthenticationService.dummyHash()` builds its throwaway hash on first use (`PasswordUtil.hash(...)`), so that first attempt runs the slow password function twice and every later one only once; a stopwatch can tell "no such user" from "wrong password" for that one try. Measured here: unknown name 124, 150, 173, 155 ms for four attempts in a row (the first is no longer the odd one); wrong password on a current hash 144 ms; on a 120,000-iteration hash 30 ms before and 150 ms now; damaged hash 146 ms. Tests in `PasswordUtilTest`: the dummy is a valid hash with `ITERATIONS` iterations (so raising `ITERATIONS` without making a new dummy fails the build), is ready at once, the padding arithmetic, what `check` reports, the constant-cost answers equal the plain ones. Not covered: the success path with an old hash also runs the upgrade (another hash) and writes to the database, which is not an existence oracle because it needs the right password; `PasswordChangeService` still uses the plain `matches` for the current password of the signed-in person.)*

[x] **[Med] The transfer-QR integrity record lives in the same folder as the picture, so whoever can swap the picture can also reset the check.** **NEW (11th scan)** `TransferQrGuard.inspect` compares the picture with `qr-transfer.sha256` next to it; if that file is missing it records the current picture and returns `FIRST_SEEN`, which is trusted silently (the only callers treat everything except `CHANGED` as fine), and any `IOException` also returns `OK`. So the attack that 2A.17 was made to stop (put your own account's QR in `data/`) only needs one more step: delete `qr-transfer.sha256` (or write the new picture's hash into it), and the next start accepts the new picture without a warning, a log line apart. The account text file `qr-transfer.info` has the same weakness. **(confirmed in code; not tried)** Keep the accepted fingerprint (and the holder/bank text) in the database (the `app_flags` style table, or a small `settings` table from 4.7) where only the app's login can change it, treat "picture present but no record" as `CHANGED` for an employee till (an admin accepts it once from the dashboard button that already exists), and treat a read error as `CHANGED`, not `OK`. *(DONE in code, Session 83: the accepted fingerprints of the picture and of the holder/bank text are kept in the database (`app_flags`, names `transferqr.pic=<sha256>` and `transferqr.info=<sha256>`, new `AppFlags.valueWithPrefix` and `replaceValues`), not in `qr-transfer.sha256`. `TransferQrGuard.State` is now `OK`, `CHANGED`, `UNCONFIRMED` (a picture nobody accepted: never recorded silently, never trusted) and `UNVERIFIABLE` (a read error of the record or the picture: no longer treated as OK), with `needsAttention()`; a changed or removed account text is a change too. `accept` and `replace` write the database and need an administrator (`accept` did not check this before). An older shop's `qr-transfer.sha256` is taken over once, only when it still matches the picture, and then deleted, so an upgrade does not ask for a re-confirmation. The payment dialog and the admin dashboard show a message for each state. Limits: on a SQLite shop the database file sits in the same data folder, so this raises the bar (editing a database instead of deleting a file) rather than closing it; on a MariaDB shop the record is on the server. Tests: rewritten `TransferQrGuardTest` (12, with an in-memory store, the old attack included) and `TransferQrGuardDatabaseTest` (2, not run here: no SQLite driver in this sandbox).)*

Not changed on purpose: `CrashHandler` and `BackupService.isDue` (see 6.65 and 3.65), and the on-screen clock, which is a display of the date and must follow the PC. Not tried: an actual change of the Windows clock on a till.)*

Anyone can type them into the user-name box (2A.39 is the same box). **(confirmed in code; a viewer that splits on U+2028 is needed to see the effect)** Replace every character that is an ISO control character or in the Unicode categories `Zl`/`Zp` with a space, keep the 40-character cut, and add the cases to a `SecurityLogTest`. *(DONE in code, Session 86: every control character, U+0080-U+009F (U+0085), U+2028 and U+2029 becomes a space; the 40-character cut stays. [x] **[Low] The backup button's `mysqldump` ignores the TLS settings.** **NEW (session 83)** `db.ssl`, `db.sslMode` and `db.sslServerCert` (2A.26) are used for the JDBC connection only. `MariaDbBackup` starts `mysqldump` with host, port, user and database, and the password in `MYSQL_PWD`, and nothing about TLS, so against a server that requires it (`REQUIRE SSL`, `require_secure_transport`) the backup button fails, and against one that does not require it the dump crosses the network in the clear even though the till's own queries are encrypted. `docs/DATABASE.md` section 7 tells the owner to put `ssl-ca=` under `[client]` in `my.ini`. **(confirmed in code; the `[client]` advice was not tried against a real server)** Pass the options to `mysqldump` too, but the two clients disagree (MariaDB's `--ssl`, `--ssl-verify-server-cert`, `--ssl-ca`; MySQL 8's `--ssl-mode=REQUIRED|VERIFY_CA|VERIFY_IDENTITY`), so first find out which one `db.mysqldump` points to (`mysqldump --version`, the same probe as 2.74) and build the arguments for that one. Test the argument builder with both version strings. *(DONE in code, Session 108: `MariaDbBackup` now passes the connection's TLS settings to `mysqldump`. When `db.ssl`/`db.sslMode` ask for encryption it first runs `mysqldump --version` (a few seconds at most; not run at all for an unencrypted connection, so nothing changes there) and builds the options of that family: MySQL gets `--ssl-mode=REQUIRED|VERIFY_CA|VERIFY_IDENTITY` and `--ssl-ca=<db.sslServerCert>`; MariaDB gets `--ssl`, `--ssl-verify-server-cert` and `--ssl-ca=`. MariaDB's program cannot check the CA without also checking the host name, so with `verify-ca` its backup is a little stricter than the connection (fails with an error, never dumps in the clear); `trust` on a new MariaDB client (11.4+, which verifies by default) may also be refused. If the program says neither "MariaDB" nor "MySQL" and TLS is wanted, **no backup is started** and the message (`db.dump.flavour.unknown`, both languages) tells the owner to write the full path of the server's own `mysqldump` in `db.mysqldump`. `docs/DATABASE.md` section 7 rewritten (the `[client]` advice is no longer needed). Tests: 10 new in `MariaDbBackupTest` (the two families told apart, every mode for both families, the CA file, refusal when unknown, password never on the command line, and end-to-end with a stand-in `mysqldump` script that answers `--version`); all 19 tests of the class passed here. **Not tried against a real server**, MariaDB or MySQL: the option spellings come from the clients' documentation.)*

[x] **[Low] In an English interface the error filter cannot tell the app's own texts from system texts.** **NEW (session 90, found while merging 2A.38 with 1.7)** `ErrorText` shows a message only if it contains Vietnamese letters (2A.38): that was enough while every text of the app was Vietnamese. With the language switch (1.7) the app's own texts can be English, and they have no Vietnamese letters. In the merged code `ErrorText.forScreen` therefore falls back to the old deny-list (`TECHNICAL` and `DRIVER_TEXT`) when the interface is not Vietnamese, so an English driver text that the lists do not know ("Read-only file system") is shown there, and any new English text of the app that happens to match a pattern ("does not exist", "not found", "Cannot ...") is hidden: `I18nTest.noMessageLooksLikeATechnicalErrorToErrorText` fails the build when that happens, which is how `csv.p.nocategory`, `startup.nosettings` and `backup.err.notwritable` had to be reworded. **(confirmed by running `ErrorTextTest`, two tests added for both interfaces)** The real fix is a marker: an exception type `UserMessage` (or a `DataAccessException` flag) for texts that come from `I18n`, and `forScreen` shows exactly those; everything else is logged and replaced by `generic()` in either language. Tests: the technical texts of `ErrorTextTest` in both interfaces. Related: 2A.38, 1.7, 5.15. *(DONE in code, found by the 2026-10-24 scan: the merged tree does not have the deny-list fallback this item describes (that was the older branch `_5_`). `ErrorText.isOwnMessage` shows a text when it has no technical pattern and either has Vietnamese letters or matches one of the texts in the language files of all languages (`I18n.LANGUAGES`, `bundleFor`, `keys`, `{n}` placeholders matching any text); everything else, in either interface, is logged and replaced by `generic()`. `ErrorTextTest.theAppsOwnEnglishTextsAreShownInEnglishAndDriverTextIsStillHidden` and `everyTextOfBothLanguageFilesThatIsAnErrorMessageIsRecognised` cover it, and `I18nTest.noMessageLooksLikeATechnicalErrorToErrorText` still guards the texts that match a technical pattern. The marker type `UserMessage` proposed above was not needed. Not run: Maven.)*

## 3. Niche and edge cases (most important first)
1. [ ] **Stock is only taken at payment.** Two open tables can both hold the last item and the second payment fails at the end; the product list on screen is
       also loaded once and can be out of date. Consider reserving stock when a line is added, or re-checking stock before the payment dialog. **(confirmed)** *(8th pass: not done: needs a design decision (reserve at add, or re-check before the payment dialog) and changes the POS screen, which cannot be loaded here.)* **Small version DONE in code, Session 132 (second task), not run and not seen on a screen; the item stays open for the big version:** pressing "Thanh toán" now first re-reads the products of the cart in one query (`ProductDAO.findByIds`, `service/StockCheck`) while the screen is locked as during a payment (`setPaymentInFlight`, so a second click cannot open two boxes), and only opens the payment box when everything is covered. Otherwise a message names each item (`"Latte: đơn có 4, chỉ còn 3."`, or `"...: đã ngừng bán."` for a product switched off or deleted meanwhile), says nothing has been charged, and the product tiles are refreshed; the cashier lowers the quantity or removes the line and presses pay again. The lines of one product are added up (as the payment does). It is a warning, not a guarantee: another till can still sell the item between the check and the click on "Xác nhận", and then the payment's own guarded `UPDATE ... stock >= ?` still fails cleanly (unchanged). If the re-read itself fails the box opens anyway. **Not done (needs decision D9, which is not in the project files):** reserving stock when a line is added, so two tables cannot both hold the last item. Tests: `StockCheckTest` 9, `StockRecheckDbTest` 3.
2. [x] **Editing a product can overwrite sales.** **NEW (2nd scan)** `ProductDAO.update` writes `stock = ?` with the number that was in the edit dialog. If a sale (another window, or a payment while the dialog is open)
       changes the stock after the dialog opened, saving the dialog puts the old number back and the sold items reappear. Fix: save stock changes as a difference
       (`stock = stock + ?`) or compare with the stock read when the dialog opened, and warn on a mismatch. **(confirmed)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** stock 10, a sale of 3 (stock 7), then saving a product object loaded before the sale (only the name changed) put the stock back to **10**. **DONE in code, 8th pass:** `Product` remembers the stock it was read with (`loadedStock`); `ProductDAO.update` writes `stock=COALESCE(?, stock)` and sends NULL when the stock number was not changed, so a rename or price edit no longer puts the old stock back over sales made while the dialog was open (the 6th-scan case: 10, sale of 3, rename: now stays 7). A stock number that was typed in is still written as typed (a stock-take); a sale made while the dialog was open is then not added back, which is what typing a counted number means. A product object that was not read from the database (new, or built in code) is still written in full. Checked: the new SQL on real SQLite (rename-only keeps 7, typed 20 gives 20, typed 0 gives 0); the changed files compile with the JDK compiler (0 errors); two tests added in `ProductDAOTest` (compile-checked, **not run**: no database driver here, so they need your `mvn test`).
3. [x] **Two copies of the app on the same database.** There is no single-instance lock, so a double-click opens a second window on the same SQLite file. **(confirmed)** **DONE in code, session 43:** two copies on one data folder: the first holds a lock on `lunavera.lock`; the second shows 'đang chạy' and exits (`SingleInstance`; checked with two real processes, and the lock frees itself when the first ends).

One rule in `OrderDAO.EFFECTIVE_DAY` (paid day, else opened day for orders never paid), used by the report summary, the dashboard counts and the history date filter; tested with an order opened 23:50 and paid 00:10. *(8th pass: the rule is now applied in every report and dashboard query; before, the best-seller, daily, profit and "today revenue" queries still used `paid_at` alone. See 3.23.)*

       (see feature 4.2, audit log). **(confirmed for the code path; intended policy is the owner's call)**

13. [x] **[Med] Cancelling a paid order changes an earlier day's totals.** **NEW (3rd scan)** `ReportDAO` counts revenue by the `paid_at` day and only for status `PAID`. A sale cancelled today but paid last week disappears from last week's *(14th pass: NOT done. The history search now compares `VN_KEY(name)` with the typed text folded the same way, through `DatabaseConfig.searchKeySql(column)` (SQLite: `VN_KEY(column)`; MariaDB: the column itself, because its `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` collation already ignores case and accents). So `nguyễn`, `NGUYỄN` and `nguyen` all find `Nguyễn`, and `dao` finds `Đào`. New `VietnameseSearchTest` (5; the first four also run on MariaDB). The folding rule was checked in Python. The name is searched with `LIKE` on the folded text, so a search is a little slower on a very large history (the function runs per row).)* *(Session 60, by `progress.md`: the data layer was compiled against the real `sqlite-jdbc` 3.46.1.3 and `VietnameseSearchTest` is not among the failures of that run, so the compile risk named above did not show up; Maven itself was still not run.)*

   *(DONE in code, Session 122: a new SQLite file is now set up in ONE transaction: `DatabaseConfig.createFreshSqliteSchema` writes every table, the later columns and `PRAGMA user_version` together, so a start killed after the first tables leaves an empty file at version 0 and the next start is a fresh set-up again. **What the old behaviour did (reproduced on SQLite 3.45 by running the real `SqliteSchema.TABLES` and the migration's own SQL):** the `users` table is the 3rd of 9 statements; killed after it, the next start saw `users` + version 0, ran `MoneyMigration` on tables that were already INTEGER, and its rebuild replaced the current definitions with the first release's (`products` lost `reorder_level` and `sort_order` until `addLaterColumns` put them back). No row can be lost this way (the crash happens before anything is stored), so the item's worry about data was unfounded, but the schema ends up different from a normal new database. Also done: `MoneyMigration.runIfNeeded` no longer rebuilds a version-0 database whose `products.price` is already INTEGER (`alreadyInIntegerDong`: it only records version 1), which repairs the leftovers an older build may already have made; and every SQLite upgrade step in `SchemaMigrations.upgrade` now runs together with its version bump in one transaction (`DatabaseConfig.runAtomically`). **Not changed:** MariaDB commits every `CREATE TABLE` itself, so a transaction cannot help there; its set-up is already safe to repeat (`IF NOT EXISTS`, version written last), and `MoneyMigration` is SQLite-only. Tests: new `SchemaSetupAtomicityTest` (11: the crash after `users` leaves no table and version 0, also seen from a new connection to the file; the next start is a complete fresh set-up; the helper rolls back tables, columns, index and version together, commits on success and joins an open transaction; a failing upgrade step leaves the old version and none of the step; a new-format version-0 file is not rebuilt and keeps `unit_cost`; the guard tells REAL from INTEGER). Compiled against the changed code; **the tests need the SQLite driver and were not run here**; the premises (DDL and `user_version` roll back together; the migration changes the definitions) were checked on a real SQLite with Python `sqlite3`.)*

[x] **[Med] The profit figures of the report are wrong for discounted orders, and old days change when a cost is edited.** **NEW (12th scan)** Item 4.20 records both limits inside a ticked note but nothing is open. `ReportDAO.profit`, `topProducts` and `dailySales` compute `line_total - quantity * products.cost` per line: (1) the order's discount is not subtracted, while "revenue" is the order total after the discount, so for a discounted order the report says revenue minus costs is less than the profit it prints; with a 20 % staff discount on a shop's busy day the "Lợi nhuận gộp" can be well above the truth; (2) the cost is read from the product row *now*, so raising a cost today lowers the profit of last month, and lines of a product whose cost was empty then and filled in later start to count. **(confirmed in code)** Store the cost on the order line when the order is paid (`order_items.unit_cost`, a `LATER_COLUMNS` entry like 2A.18's, in both schemas and the importer, copied from `products.cost` in `OrderDAO`), compute profit from it, and spread the order discount over its lines in proportion to `line_total` (or subtract the order's discount once per order). Keep "-" for lines with no cost known at the time. Tests: a discounted order whose revenue minus cost equals the printed profit; a cost edit that does not move an earlier day. *(14th pass: DONE in code. New column `order_items.unit_cost` (`SqliteSchema.LATER_COLUMNS`, `MariaDbSchema`, the importer's column list), set by `OrderDAO.stampUnitCosts` inside the payment's own transaction (both `insert` of a paid order and `payExistingOrder`) from `products.cost`; null means not known then. `ReportDAO.profit`, `topProducts` and `dailySales` now use it and subtract each line's share of the order discount (`discount x line_total / subtotal`, summed and rounded once), so revenue minus costs equals the printed profit and a later cost edit does not move an earlier day; lines with no cost then stay \"not known\" even if a cost is entered later. A one-time job (`DatabaseConfig.backfillUnitCostOnce`, flag `order_items_unit_cost_v1`, after the import) gives the lines of orders paid or refunded before the upgrade their product's cost today, so the history does not go blank; open orders and products with no cost stay null, and it never runs again. The SQL was run on real SQLite with the project's own table definitions (discounted order 47,000 = 72,000 - 25,000; a cost edit leaves the day unchanged; the no-cost line is excluded; the backfill touches only paid and refunded lines). 4 new `Section4ReportsTest` cases and `UnitCostBackfillTest` (2), compiled, NOT run (need the driver). **Notes:** the profit of the days before the upgrade is only as good as today's costs; the importer expects its SQLite file to have the column like the other later columns (a file from before this version must be opened by this version once first).)*

55. [x] **[Low] The same names are different on SQLite but the same on MariaDB.** **NEW (13th scan)** `MariaDbSchema` builds every table with `COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, which ignores case **and accents**, and `categories.name` is `UNIQUE` (line 63); on SQLite the same column is a plain `UNIQUE` text (`SqliteSchema` line 56). On MariaDB the categories "Trà" and "Tra" (or "Cà phê" and "Ca phe") are one name, on SQLite they are two, and the product-name check in the app (`existsByNameInCategory`) answers differently on the two databases. Copying a SQLite shop into MariaDB (`SqliteToMariaDbImporter`) can therefore stop on a duplicate key for data that was valid before. **(confirmed from the two schema files; a failing import was not tried)** Decide one rule (accent-insensitive on both is the friendlier one, see 3.18 and 1.9), make the SQLite side follow it with the `VN_KEY` function, and let the importer report the two clashing names instead of failing half-way. **DONE in code, session 123.** *Rule decided: accent-insensitive on both, exactly MariaDB's own rule (case, accents and trailing spaces do not count; spaces inside a name do; `đ` is its own letter).* **Checked on a real MariaDB 10.11 first:** `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` has `Trà` = `Tra` and `Cà phê` = `Ca phe`, but `Đỗ` is **not** `Do` (so the `VN_KEY` / `TextNormalizer.searchKey` fold the item suggested, which turns `đ` into `d`, would have been stricter than the server and is kept for search boxes only), and it ignores trailing spaces but not double spaces inside a name. `TextNormalizer.collationKey` is that rule in Java; it was compared with the server for 703 spellings of every Vietnamese letter in both cases, composed and with separate marks (12 614 equal pairs on both sides, 0 differences) and for 2 704 ordered pairs of names inserted into a `categories` table made like `MariaDbSchema`'s (0 differences). **What changed:** (1) `CategoryDAO.rejectDuplicateName` uses `TextNormalizer.uniqueKey` (`collationKey` of `key`, a little stricter than the server because extra spaces never make another name, 3.18), so `Trà` / `Tra` is refused with the same plain message on both databases; before, SQLite took both and MariaDB answered with the server's own duplicate-key text. The SQLite side is the code check on purpose and **not** a `VN_KEY` unique index (3.18, 14th pass: an index on a function breaks every other tool that opens the file, and the function must exist on every connection). (2) `SqliteToMariaDbImporter.checkSource` now looks at `categories` before one row is copied (`clashingCategoryNames`, compared with `collationKey`, so a file MariaDB would accept is never refused) and says which two names clash (`db.import.dupcategory`, both languages); a failing copy half-way is no longer possible for this cause. **Correction to the item:** the product-name check does *not* differ between the databases: `ProductDAO.existsByNameInCategory` compares in Java on both and `products.name` has no unique key, so products stay as they are (accent-sensitive, `ProductNameCheckTest` line 66 says so on purpose); only `categories.name` had the difference. **Tests:** `TextNormalizerTest` +4, `SqliteImportSourceCheckTest` +3, new `MariaDbCategoryNameRuleTest` (1, MariaDB-only: every pair of 52 spellings, server verdict against `collationKey`). **Not done:** a SQLite shop that already holds `Trà` and `Tra` keeps both until the owner renames one (the app cannot merge two categories); only the import refuses it, with the two names.

The history search only understands digits without a leading zero and `#12`, and the screens show the order as `#12` (5.12). **Reproduced on the real database code:** `DH000012`, `000012` and `# 12` all return 0 rows for an existing order. The accountant must convert by hand. Put the reference in one helper next to `OrderNumber` (`transferNote(id)` and its reverse) and let the history search accept `DH000012` and `#12` alike. Related: 4.31. *(DONE in code, session 89: `OrderNumber.transferNote(id)` writes `DH000012` (the payment dialog uses it) and `OrderNumber.parseReference` reads `12`, `#12`, `# 12`, `DH000012`, `dh 000012` and `000012`; `OrderDAO`'s history filter uses it, so the bank-statement reference finds the order. Plain digits with a leading zero other than exactly six stay a phone search. New `OrderNumberTest` (run in a plain JDK); no database test of the search itself.)*

Normalise to NFC (`java.text.Normalizer`) in `hash` and `matches` (hashes of plain ASCII passwords stay valid), or say in the password rule that letters without accents are required. Related: 3.18. *(DONE in code, session 89: `PasswordUtil.hash` and the check normalise to NFC, so the composed and decomposed spelling of an accented password match. A hash made earlier from a password typed in the decomposed form is tried as typed as well, so nobody is locked out. Two new tests in `PasswordUtilTest` (run in a plain JDK).)*

[x] **[Low] There is no sensible upper limit on a price or a quantity, and the loyalty points wrap round to a negative number above 21 trillion dong.** **NEW (16th scan)** The only limit on a price is the 15 digits of `MoneyParser` (999 trillion dong), a quantity has none (`Cart.setQuantity` takes any `int`; 5.27 will add a typed box), and `LoyaltyPolicy.pointsFor` ends with `.intValue()`. **Reproduced by running the classes:** an order total of 25,000,000,000,000 gives **-1,794,967,296** points, 21,474,836,480,000 gives -2,147,483,648, and `Cart.totalQuantity()` of two lines of 2,000,000,000 gives -294,967,296. A price of 999,999,999,999,999 times 9,000 is stopped cleanly by `Money.toDong` ("Số tiền quá lớn hoặc không hợp lệ"), so only the points wrap silently, but a price typed with three extra zeros (an easy slip, 2.77 shows others) is saved without any question and every later sale of it is odd. **(confirmed by running the code)** Ask the owner for a ceiling (for example 100,000,000đ for a price and 999 for a quantity of one line), check it in the product form, `ProductService` and the CSV import, `Cart.setQuantity` and the typed box of 5.27; make `pointsFor` clamp with `Math.toIntExact` guarded by a `min`; make `totalQuantity` a `long` or stop at the cap. Tests with the numbers above. *(DONE in code, session 107. **The ceilings are my choice, the item's own suggestions; change them in `service/Limits`:** a price or cost at most 100.000.000đ, a stock at most 1.000.000, a cart line at most 999 pieces. Checked in the product form (own messages), the CSV import (a problem line), `ProductDAO.bind` (last line of defence) and `Cart` (`LINE_LIMIT` outcome with a warning at the till; `setQuantity` holds the ceiling). `LoyaltyPolicy.pointsFor` clamps to `Integer.MAX_VALUE` instead of wrapping negative; `Cart.totalQuantity` is a `long`. Prices already stored above the ceiling still sell; they are refused only when edited. The typed quantity box of 5.27 does not exist yet: it must call `Cart.exceedsLineLimit`. Tests: `LimitsTest`, 2 in `CartTest`, 1 in `LoyaltyPolicyTest` (the numbers of the item), 1 in `ProductCsvTest` (all run in a plain JDK).)*

Cần có các cột ..." although the three columns are there. **(reproduced by running `ProductCsv.parse`)** Fix: also try the tab as a separator when the header has no comma or semicolon, or say "tệp phân cách bằng dấu tab: hãy lưu lại dạng CSV UTF-8". *(DONE in code, session 107: `ProductCsv.delimiterOf` picks the most used of comma, semicolon and tab in the header line, and `ProductCsv.decode` reads a file that starts with a UTF-16 byte-order mark (Excel's "Unicode Text") as UTF-16; the import button uses it. 3 new tests in `ProductCsvTest` (run in a plain JDK).)*

62. [x] **[Low] The SQLite-to-MariaDB import opens the old file read-write and never looks at its version or its health.** **NEW (22nd scan)** `SqliteToMariaDbImporter.importIfRequested` opens `jdbc:sqlite:<file>` with the default (read-write) mode although its comment promises the file "is only read, never changed": opening a file with a leftover `-wal`/`-journal` lets SQLite replay or delete it, and a file that is not a Lunavera one but is a database is still touched. It checks only that a `users` table exists. It does not read `PRAGMA user_version`: a file from a **newer** app version carries columns and meanings this build ignores (they are dropped without a word), a file from before the money migration (version 0) holds `REAL` prices that MariaDB rounds into whole dong, and it does not run `PRAGMA integrity_check` / `foreign_key_check` although the foreign keys are switched off for the copy, so orphan rows (an order line whose product is gone) are copied and only fail later on the first screen that joins them. **(confirmed in code; not run, no MariaDB here)** Open the file read-only (`SQLiteConfig.setReadOnly(true)`), refuse a version above `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` with a message, warn on version 0, run `integrity_check` and `foreign_key_check` before the copy and stop with the first problem rows; test with a hand-made file for each case. **DONE in code (Session 109):** `SqliteToMariaDbImporter.openSourceReadOnly` (`SQLiteConfig.setReadOnly(true)`) and `checkSource`, run after the "has a `users` table" test and before the first row is copied. A version above `SchemaMigrations.LATEST_VERSION` (2; not `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`, which is 1 and is only the money migration, so a valid version-2 file would have been refused) stops the import with `db.import.newer`; a failed `integrity_check`, or a file that cannot be read to the end, stops it with `db.import.damaged` (SQLite's own text goes to the log only, so the screen filter does not hide the message); rows found by `foreign_key_check` stop it with `db.import.orphans` (the count and the first 5 as `table #row -> parent`); version 0 is copied with a warning in the log only, because the SQLite version of the app upgrades such a file losslessly. A refusal throws like any failed import, so the app stops with the message instead of carrying on with an empty shop. Tests: `SqliteImportSourceCheckTest` (8, files made by hand, including a damaged page and a "file is byte for byte unchanged" check). `docs/DATABASE.md` says so. Compiled against a stand-in for `SQLiteConfig`; **not run**. Not tested: a leftover `-wal` or `-journal` file next to the old file.

63. [x] **[Low] A backup is never opened again after it is written, so a bad one is found only on the day it is needed.** **NEW (24th scan)** `BackupService.createBackup` takes the database copy (`VACUUM INTO`), writes the zip to `*.part` and moves it into place; nothing then reads the zip back, although the code to do so exists (`inspect`, which reads every entry so the CRCs are checked and runs `integrity_check` on the database inside). A failing disk, a USB stick pulled out just after the move or a full cache can leave a zip that looks fine in the folder; the 14 automatic copies are then all equally bad, and the first sign is the restore that fails. **(confirmed in code)** After the move, run `inspect(zip)` (a few hundred milliseconds on a shop-size file), delete and report a copy that fails ("Bản sao lưu vừa tạo bị lỗi"), and say "đã kiểm tra" in the success message; test with a zip truncated by a stand-in writer. *(DONE in code, session 89: after the move into place `createBackup` reads the zip back with `verifyZip` (the zip's index and every entry's CRC; for a SQLite shop also `inspect`, i.e. `integrity_check`); a copy that fails is deleted and reported ("Bản sao lưu vừa tạo bị lỗi...", key `backup.verify.failed`). A passphrase backup is also decrypted and checked. The success message now says "Đã kiểm tra". The automatic backup goes through the same code. New tests: a truncated zip fails, a fresh backup is kept. NOT run: they need the database driver.)*

[x] **[Low] One damaged "hire date" in a staff record stops that person logging in and empties the whole account list.** **NEW (25th scan)** `EmployeeDAO.map` reads `hire_date` with a bare `LocalDate.parse(...)` (the customer and order code use the tolerant `DbTime.parse`). A value that is not `yyyy-MM-dd` (a date typed into phpMyAdmin as `18/10/2026`, a date-time text left by an import, an empty-looking space) throws `DateTimeParseException`, which is not a `DataAccessException`: `reloadAccounts` fails with a generic error so the admin sees **no accounts at all**, and the login of that employee fails at "Không thể tải thông tin nhân viên" (`LoginController.onLoginSuccess` -> `employeeDAO.findById`), which looks like a wrong password to them. **(confirmed in code; not run)** Parse the column defensively (take the first 10 characters, return `null` and log a warning when it cannot be read), and add the three bad values to `EmployeeDAOTest`; the same few lines are worth a look in every `map` that parses text. *(DONE in code, session 89: `EmployeeDAO.readHireDate` reads the first 10 characters, and returns no date plus a warning with the staff id (never the text) when it cannot be read. Two new tests in `EmployeeDetailsTest`, one writing `18/10/2026` into the table. NOT run: the DB one needs the driver. The other `LocalDate.parse` calls (`OrderDAO` line ~889, `ReportDAO`) read values the SQL itself produced with `DATE(...)`, so they were left.)*

65. [x] **[Med] After the computer's clock has been wrong, no automatic backup is made until the clock catches up with the wrong date.** **NEW (session 84)** `BackupService.isDue` is "no automatic backup yet, or the newest one is at least 24 hours old", with the age taken as `Duration.between(newestFileTime, now)`. A backup made while the clock was ahead (a till whose date was set to next year, a wrong day after a flat battery, a clock set forward to test something) has a modified time in the future, so the age is negative and the answer stays "not due" until the real date reaches it, which can be months or years; meanwhile `pruneAutomatic` keeps the newest-by-modified-time, which is that future file, and deletes the real recent ones first as the count goes over 14. **(confirmed in code; not run, but the arithmetic is the whole check)** Treat a newest backup that is more than a few minutes in the future as "not a usable date": due now. Order the backups for pruning by the stamp in the file name (`auto-yyyyMMdd-HHmmss`), which the program wrote itself, or by the oldest of name and modified time, so one wrong date cannot make a file immortal. Tests with a file whose time is 400 days ahead: `isDue` is true, and `pruneAutomatic` removes the wrong-dated one only after the older real ones are over the limit, or first (decide, and test that). *(DONE in code, session 89: `isDue` ignores an automatic backup whose file time is more than 10 minutes ahead of the clock, so a wrong-clock copy no longer blocks the next backup. Pruning ranks a backup whose name stamp is in the future as the oldest, so it is deleted first once there are over 14 and never outlives the real recent ones (decision: wrong-dated first). Tests: 400 days ahead gives `isDue` true; two prune tests with an injected `now`. NOT run: they need the database driver.)*

## 4. Features to add (most important first)

> **8th pass, status of the open features.** Done here: 4.1 (found already complete) and the `before-restore` part of 4.28. Everything else still open needs at least one of: a **new screen or dialog** (JavaFX cannot be loaded in the environment these passes run in, so a screen could not be seen or clicked), **new tables or columns in both database definitions** plus the importer, a numbered migration and tests on both databases (cannot be run there), or a **decision from the owner / an accountant**. Grouped: screens + new tables: 4.2 audit log, 4.5 stock receiving log, 4.6 shift close, 4.9 order notes, 4.10 table transfer / merge / split bill, 4.18 table management, 4.25 take-away orders, 4.31 transfer reference; screens only: 4.7 settings, 4.8 merge customers, 4.12 employee screen, 4.15 first-run wizard (the admin-account part exists, see 2A.5), 4.16 spending points, 4.19 reorder level (needs a column), 4.24 database health line, 4.26 set the transfer QR in the app, 4.27 clean up unused pictures, 4.29 former staff; owner or accountant first: 4.11 promotions, 4.22 Vietnamese invoicing rules; hardware: 4.13 kitchen ticket printer; 4.14 several tills: the MariaDB mode (1.5) is the base for it, but two tills at once have not been tried. What would let more of these be built and checked: allow `repo.maven.apache.org` in the sandbox network settings so `mvn clean test` runs, and give a way to start the app with a display (or accept that screens are checked by you).

16. [x] **[Med] Spending loyalty points.** **NEW (3rd scan)** Points are earned and shown with a tier, but nothing in the payment flow can use them, so they have no value for the customer. Add a redemption (for example 1 point = 1.000đ off), recorded
        on the order and the customer's balance in the same transaction as the payment, with the cancel-order rule in edge case 3.7 settled first. **(confirmed: no redemption code found)** **DONE in code, session 135 (rule D5 was not written down anywhere in the project files, so this is the rule I applied: change a number in `LoyaltyPolicy` to change it).** *The rule:* **1 point = 1.000đ off** (`VND_PER_POINT_SPENT`; points are still earned at 1 per 10.000đ paid, so the customer gets 10% back); points may pay **at most 50% of the bill after the cashier's discount** (`MAX_PERCENT_OF_BILL`, whole points, so a sale is never paid entirely with points and no total can reach 0); only a **customer attached to the sale** can spend (typing a new name and phone at the till makes a customer with 0 points); **earned points are worked out on what was actually paid** (after the points); a **refund gives the spent points back** (see 3.7 below). *Payment dialog:* a "Dùng điểm" row under the discount: the customer's points (read again from the database when the dialog opens), the rate, the most that can be used, a "Tối đa" button; the total, change and quick-cash buttons follow what is typed; a number that is not allowed (more than they have, more than half the bill, not a whole number) stops the payment with the reason in plain words; with no customer attached or no points the row says so. *Saved:* `orders.points_redeemed` (how many) and `orders.points_value` (what they were worth then, so a later change of the rate does not rewrite old orders); the value is **also inside `orders.discount`** and the reason says "Dùng n điểm" (after the cashier's own reason, if any), so `total = subtotal - discount` still holds and the reports, the profit allocation, the discounts total and the receipt's discount line needed no change; the printed receipt has a "Điểm đã dùng: n" line. *Transaction:* `OrderDAO.insert` and `payExistingOrder` take the points with `UPDATE customers SET loyalty_points = loyalty_points - ? WHERE id = ? AND loyalty_points >= ? AND anonymised_at IS NULL` in the same transaction as the sale: when another till spent them first, nothing is saved (stock, order and balance all as before) and the message says so. `CheckoutService` checks the same rule first with the plain-words messages and applies the staff discount limit (2.3) to the cashier's own discount **only**: the customer's points are not the cashier's to give. *Refund (the rule 3.7 needed settled):* `cancelPaidOrder` takes back the points the order earned (floor 0, as decided in session 43) **and then** returns the points it spent (capped at the largest number the column holds); a customer made anonymous since gets nothing back (their record keeps no points, 4.8). *Both databases:* two columns through the existing later-columns list (SQLite and MariaDB alike), the MariaDB `CREATE TABLE`, and the importer; the guarded spend and give-back statements were run on a real MariaDB 10.11 (1 row when allowed, 0 rows when refused, capped, 0 rows for an anonymous record). *Tests (20 new):* `LoyaltyRedemptionRulesTest` (6, no database), `PointsRedemptionTest` (14: spent and recorded, no points leaves the columns empty, staff limit does not apply, a cashier's discount plus points, more than the balance, more than half the bill, no customer and negative, another till spent them first, an open order, refund gives them back, refund floor, refund without points, refund for an anonymous customer, profit and discounts in the report); `LaterColumnsExistTest` lists the two columns. *Not done / limits:* no setting screen for the rate or the 50% (a code change); no "points" column in the order history list or the CSV (the reason text says it); the dashboard; a loyalty cost line in the report (the discount total includes it); the sales screen shows the customer's points only in the dialog; the payment dialog and the receipt were parsed and read, not opened (JavaFX is not available here), so the new row is for you to look at.

A table of four who pay part in cash and part by transfer has to be recorded as one method, so the "revenue by method" line (4.30) and the cash the shift close should find in the drawer (4.6, done in Session 127: it counts the paid orders whose method is cash) are wrong for that order. **(confirmed in code)** Needs an `order_payments` table (order id, method, amount) with the order's total as the sum; the payment dialog gets a second line. **DONE in code (Session 135), with rule D4 as chosen here (change it if the owner wants otherwise):** *two methods means cash plus one other method* (card, transfer or an e-wallet), nothing else (no card plus transfer, no three ways). New table `order_payments` (order id, method, amount; one row per method, written only for such an order, in the same transaction as the payment; the two amounts add up to the total); an order paid with one method has no rows, exactly as before. `orders.payment_method` of a split order holds the non-cash method (and its reference), so every old reader still shows a sensible value. The payment box has a tick "Một phần trả bằng tiền mặt" (`SplitPaymentPanel`, shown while a non-cash method is chosen): the cash part is typed, the rest goes to the method chosen, and the transfer QR and the e-wallet block show only the rest. The cash part is taken as exact (no "tiền khách đưa" or change for a split). Readers changed: receipt window and thermal receipt ("Tiền mặt 30.000 + Chuyển khoản 70.000", `PaymentText`/`ReceiptText.paymentOf`), history column and orders CSV (`PaymentText.of`), the report's payment lines (a split order counts once under each method with that method's amount; the lines add up to the revenue), the shift close's cash and the cash share per person (`ShiftDAO.CASH_OF_ORDER`: only the cash line of a split order). Refund needs nothing (the lines follow the order's status). Schema: `order_payments` in `SqliteSchema`, `MariaDbSchema` (+`DROP_ORDER`) and the SQLite-to-MariaDB importer. Tests: `SplitPaymentTest` (9). Not seen: the payment box on a screen.

33. [x] **Waste and breakage log ("hủy hàng").** **NEW (8th scan)** Stock only goes down through a sale and only goes up through a refund or by typing a new number in the product dialog (`ProductDAO`), so milk that spoiled or a cup that was dropped is invisible: stock is fixed by hand and the loss shows in no report. **(confirmed: no table or screen records it)** A small "Hao hụt" action per product (quantity, reason, who, when) that lowers stock and shows in the report as a cost, together with 4.5 (receiving log, done in Session 127: `stock_log`, `StockLogDAO`, `StockLogDialog`: a waste line can be a third kind of line there, lowering the stock in the same transaction) and 2.66 (refund restock). **DONE in code, session 132.** A third kind of stock log line, `StockLogEntry.WASTE`, written by `StockLogDAO.writeOff(productId, quantity, reason)`: the stock is lowered with `stock = stock - ? ... AND stock >= ?` and the line is written in **one transaction** (nothing changes when either fails); the quantity is stored negative (`stockBefore` → `stockAfter` reads like the other lines), the **reason is required** (cleaned like the other free text, at most 100 characters, kept in the existing `note` column), **who and when** come from the session and `AppClock` like every log line, and `unit_cost` holds the **product's cost price at that moment** so the loss does not change when the price is edited later (a product with no cost price gives a line with no cost, which the report counts apart and never prices at 0, as in 4.20). More than the stock is refused with the number in stock (also when a sale at another till takes the last pieces in between); all of the stock may be written off; administrators only, like the rest of the log. **No schema change, no migration** (`stock_log.kind` is free text; the importer and both schemas are untouched). **Screen:** the "Nhập kho" dialog (`StockLogDialog`) has a switch "Nhập hàng / Hao hụt" over the same form: in waste mode the cost, supplier and note rows give way to a reason box (four suggested reasons, own words allowed), the button reads "Ghi hao hụt", the status line says what the loss cost, and the log table shows the new type "Hao hụt" next to receipts and edits. **Report:** `ReportDAO.waste(from, to)` (lines, pieces, cost, lines without a cost price; by the day of the log line) and `WasteText`: a line under the summary of the report window ("Hao hụt: n dòng, n sản phẩm, chi phí X", with the note about lines without a cost price; left out when there was none) and three rows in the CSV (always written). The **gross profit above it is before waste** (waste is a loss on top of what was sold; the figure is not reduced). **Checked on a real MariaDB 10.11:** the SQL of `ReportDAO.waste` on a `stock_log` made like `MariaDbSchema`'s gave 3 lines, 15 pieces, 29 000 đ, 1 without a cost for a day with the edges 23:59:59 in and 00:00:00 out, other kinds and other days left out; on SQLite the same through `WasteLogTest`. **Tests (15 new):** `WasteLogTest` (11: the line, the cost of that moment, no cost, too many, reason and quantity rules, long reason, product gone, administrators only, the report's lines/pieces/cost, day edges and other kinds, an empty range, profit unchanged), `WasteTextTest` (4). **Not done:** waste per ingredient (4.41); waste by an employee who is not an administrator (the dialog is on the product screen, which is the administrator's); a fixed list of reasons with a total per reason (the reason is free text in `note`, so the report shows one total, not one per reason; a `reason` column would need schema version 4); the dashboard; the refund-restock line (2.66) is not touched. The dialog and the report window were parsed and read, not opened (JavaFX is not available here), so the switch and the new report line are for you to look at.

[x] **Separate e-wallet methods (MoMo, ZaloPay, VNPay) in the reports.** **NEW (8th scan, idea)** Most Vietnamese cafes take wallet QR payments, and today they must be recorded as "Chuyển khoản", which mixes them with bank transfers when the bank statement is checked. **(reasoned)** Add them as extra `PaymentMethod` values (the column is plain text, so no schema change) and show them in 4.30. **DONE in code, session 124.** `PaymentMethod` has `MOMO`, `ZALOPAY` and `VNPAY` (stored as the enum name, 7 characters at most, so the `VARCHAR(20)` column of MariaDB and the plain text of SQLite need nothing; no migration; old rows read as before; the labels are brand names, the same in both languages). `PaymentMethod.eWallets()`, `isEWallet()` and `needsReceivedConfirmation()` (bank transfer and the three wallets) are the one place that says which methods need the "money arrived" tick and may keep a code. **Payment dialog:** a second row of radio buttons with the three wallets; choosing one shows the amount, an optional "wallet transaction code" and the tick "I have received the full amount in the e-wallet", and the button reads "Đã nhận tiền - hoàn tất" as for a transfer; finishing without the tick is refused with the wallet's name in the message. The shop's **bank QR picture is not shown** for a wallet (it belongs to the bank account). **Saved and shown:** the code goes into `orders.transfer_ref` (cleaned like a bank reference, `OrderDAO.paymentDetails`; a cash figure is never kept for a wallet), is searchable in the history like a bank reference, and is printed on the receipt as "Reference: ..." (`ReceiptText.paymentExtras`). **Reports (4.30):** `ReportDAO.paymentSplit` groups by `payment_method`, so each wallet is its own line (orders and revenue) in the report window and its CSV, adding up to the total; it needed no change. **Tests (7 new):** `PaymentMethodTest` +3, `ReceiptPaymentExtrasTest` +1, `OrderPaymentDetailsTest` +2 (each wallet saved and read back, an open order paid with a wallet), `Section4ReportsTest` +1 (bank transfer and wallets are separate lines); `I18nTest` lists the three brand names as the same text in both languages on purpose. **Not done / limits:** no wallet has its own QR picture or account text (the guard in `TransferQrGuard` is only for the bank QR); nothing checks the payment with the wallet (the cashier ticks that it arrived, as for a transfer); the dashboard does not show the split (same as 4.30); a wallet that is not one of the three needs a new enum value (a code change), there is no settings screen for the list. The payment dialog was not opened here (JavaFX is not available): it was parsed and read, not run, so the layout of the second radio row is for you to look at.

[x] **[Med] A second place for the backups (a USB stick, another PC, a synced folder).** **NEW (11th scan)** The daily automatic zip goes to `data/backups/`, on the same disk as the database; `BackupService` says so in a note and the manual backup warns when the zip is inside `data/`, but nothing copies a backup anywhere else, and nothing tells the owner when the newest copy outside the PC is old. A dead disk, a stolen PC or ransomware on the till takes the data and every backup together. 5.18 asks only for a reminder at closing time. **(confirmed in code)** Add a setting "Thư mục sao lưu thứ hai" (4.7): after each automatic backup copy the zip there (a failure is shown on the dashboard, not only logged), and show "Bản sao lưu ngoài máy gần nhất: N ngày trước" with a red state after 3 days. Check the target is not inside `data/` and is writable before accepting it. *(DONE in code, session 112: dashboard button "Nơi sao lưu thứ hai" (admin) picks a folder with a `DirectoryChooser`; `SecondBackupFolder.problem` refuses a folder that does not exist, is the data folder or inside it, or cannot be written (a probe file). The setting is stored per till in `ui-settings.properties` (a USB stick belongs to one PC), not in the shared database; the 4.7 settings screen does not exist yet, so the button is the "setting". After every automatic backup, and at the hourly check, `SecondBackupFolder.catchUp` copies the newest `auto-*.zip` there if it is not there yet (through a `.part` file, size checked, newest 14 kept, other files untouched), so a stick plugged in later still gets it. The dashboard health box has a line "Bản sao lưu ngoài máy gần nhất: N ngày trước", in the warning colour after 3 days, when no copy was ever made or when the last copy failed (the error text is shown; the next hourly check retries). Tests: `SecondBackupFolderTest` (10, run here); the copy, the stick pulled out and plugged back in, and the setting were also run in a plain JDK against the real class. Not done: copying manual backups; encrypting the copy; a second folder shared by all tills (it is per till on purpose); the line is read from what this till last did, it does not look at the stick.)*

36. [x] **[Med] Lock the till and switch cashier with a short PIN.** **NEW (11th scan)** The only ways to change who is selling are a full logout and a login with the password (600,000-round hash, throttled), and the idle logout after 15 minutes closes the screen and every dialog, which the cart autosave survives but the cashier's patience may not. In a busy cafe two or three people share one till; they tend to stay signed in as one account, and then `orders.paid_by` (2.47) and every discount reason name the wrong person. **(reasoned)** A "Khóa" button (and the idle timeout) that locks the screen while keeping the cart, and a 4-6 digit PIN per account (stored hashed, with its own throttle) to unlock or to switch; the PIN must never be enough to sign in from the login screen, and an admin screen stays behind the password. **DONE in code (Session 136), with rule D7 as chosen here (change it if the owner wants otherwise):** (1) only *staff* accounts have a PIN; an administrator never does, an administrator's till opens only with the administrator's password, and a PIN can never take over an administrator's session; (2) **a PIN never signs anybody in**: `PinService` is not used by the login screen (a test reads `LoginController` and `AuthenticationService` and fails if they mention it; a PIN typed at the login is a wrong password) and it refuses to work unless somebody is already signed in with a password as staff; (3) 4 to 6 digits, not all the same and not a run up or down (`PinRules`), set by the person themselves in the sidebar ("Mã PIN", `PinSetupDialog`) after giving the password, stored hashed like a password in `users.pin_hash`; (4) its own throttle (`LoginThrottle.persistent("pin")`, one wait per account, kept in the database) and a hard limit: after 5 wrong PINs in a row (`users.pin_failures`) the PIN is switched off and must be set again after a password sign-in, so the 10 000 four-digit PINs cannot be tried at the till; (5) an administrator resetting a password, or a former member of staff being made anonymous, also removes the PIN; (6) "Khóa" in every sidebar locks the till: `TillLock` hides and disables the content of every window (the screen is not closed, so the cart and the open order stay as they are) and `LockScreen` waits for the PIN (a list of the cashiers with a PIN, the locker first: the same person unlocks, another cashier takes over and `paid_by` and the discount reasons then name that cashier) or, for an administrator or a staff member without a PIN, for the password; "Đăng xuất" is the other way out, Esc and the window's X do nothing; (7) the idle timeout locks instead of signing out; a till still locked after another idle period is signed out as before. Schema: `users.pin_hash` and `users.pin_failures` (later columns on both databases, `MariaDbSchema`, importer). Tests: `PinRulesTest`, `PinServiceTest` (14, real SQLite), `TillLockTest`. **NOT seen:** the lock screen, the two sidebar buttons and the PIN box on a screen; the PIN check (PBKDF2) runs on the screen thread for a fraction of a second. Not done: a button for the owner to remove somebody's PIN (resetting the password does it); a PIN for the admin is refused by design.

[x] **[Med] Expenses and cash taken out of the drawer are not recorded, so "profit" is only sales minus product cost.** **NEW (13th scan)** `ReportDAO.profit` is gross profit by definition (4.20): rent, wages, electricity, milk bought on the day and cash taken out of the drawer appear nowhere (searched the code for an `expenses` table, a screen or the word "chi phí": nothing). A shop can look profitable in the report and still lose money. **(confirmed: nothing records them)** A small `expenses` table (date, amount, category, note, who) with a screen for the administrator, the lines "Chi phí" and "Lợi nhuận ròng" in the report, and the cash taken out counted in the shift close (4.6). **DONE in code, Session 134, not run and not seen on a screen:** table `expenses` (day, whole-dong amount, category, note, from_drawer, who, when written) on both databases, `shift_closes.cash_out` (later column), both in the importer; `ExpenseRules` (rules, decision D8 as built, net profit), `ExpenseDAO`, `ReportDAO.expenses`, `ExpenseText`; "Chi phí" window for administrators (sidebar button: write an expense, list a range with its total, delete a mistake) and a small "Ghi chi từ két" box in "Chốt ca" for any signed-in person; the report shows "Chi phí" and "Lợi nhuận ròng" (gross profit - waste - expenses, with the by-category line) and the CSV has the total, the six categories and the net profit; the shift close takes the cash taken out of the drawer off the expected cash and stores it (`ShiftClose.cashOut`, a column in the shift list of the report). **Decision D8 (it is not in the project files; this is the rule built, change it if yours differs):** six fixed categories (rent, wages, electricity/water/internet, supplies, equipment and repairs, other); an administrator writes any expense and deletes one; any signed-in person may only write down cash taken out of the drawer, always dated today, and cannot change or delete it; a report counts an expense on the day typed on it, a shift close counts a drawer cash-out in the shift during which it was written down; **the cost of ingredients already in a product's cost price must not be entered again** (the hint on the window says so), or it is taken off twice. Tests: `ExpenseTest` 7, `ExpenseRulesTest` 5, `ExpenseTextTest` 3, `ShiftDAOTest` +3, `ShiftCashTest` +1, `LaterColumnsExistTest` lists `shift_closes.cash_out`.

The data is all there (`orders.paid_at`, `order_items` joined to `products.category_id`). **(reasoned)** Add three small queries (group by hour of `paid_at`, by weekday, by category) and a "so với kỳ trước" figure (the same number of days just before the range) to `ReportWindow`; keep them as `ReportDAO` records with tests on a fixed day, as the existing ones do. Above the tables: busiest hour and best weekday. The previous period is the same number of days ending the day before `from` (`SalesPatterns.previousRange`). Hours and weekdays are computed in Java (`SalesPatterns`, from `ReportDAO.paidOrders`), not with SQL date functions, so SQLite and MariaDB agree. The CSV export has the previous period's figures and the hour, weekday and category tables. Tests: `SalesPatternsTest` (9) and `SalesPatternsTextTest` (5), run here; `ReportPatternsDaoTest` (3, needs the SQLite driver, not run); the two new SQL statements were run by hand on MariaDB 10.11 and gave the expected rows. 26. [x] **Warn when Caps Lock is on at the login and password fields.** **NEW (8th scan)** A cashier who types the password with Caps Lock on gets "wrong password", and every wrong try also feeds the login delay (2A.7). Nothing in `src` reads the Caps Lock state (searched). JavaFX has no public call for the key's state: use AWT's `Toolkit.getLockingKeyState(KeyEvent.VK_CAPS_LOCK)` (needs the `java.desktop` module) or watch the typed letters (a capital letter without Shift); show a small "Caps Lock đang bật" line under the field.
   Done (session 106): a "Caps Lock is on" line (orange, hidden and taking no room until needed) under the username/password fields on the login screen and in the password-change, first-run, and backup-passphrase dialogs. JavaFX 21 cannot read the key's state, so `CapsLockState` works it out from typed letters (a capital without Shift, or a small letter with Shift, means on; the Caps Lock key flips a known state; unknown until a letter is typed and again when the window loses focus; Ctrl/Alt/Meta keys and non-A-Z letters are ignored). `CapsLockWarning` wires it to the fields. Tests: `CapsLockStateTest` (6, run here). Not done: the account create/edit form (an admin typing someone else's password, no failed-attempt cost), and nothing is shown while the password is revealed with the eye button (the plain text field is a different control). The screens themselves were not run (no JavaFX here).

[x] **[Low] The money boxes refuse the way Vietnamese shops write amounts: "50k", "1tr", "50 nghìn".** **NEW (21st scan)** `MoneyParser.parseDong` accepts digits with dots, commas or spaces and a trailing `đ`/`d`/`VND`, so `50.000`, `50,000`, `50 000` and `50.000đ` work (all run on the real class), but `50k`, `50K`, `1tr`, `1,5tr`, `1 triệu` and `50 nghìn` all end in "Vui lòng nhập một số hợp lệ". Cashiers and owners write prices and cash received exactly like that every day (the payment dialog even has 50k / 100k / 200k buttons, item 5.1). **(reproduced by running `MoneyParser.parseDong` on 23 inputs)** Accept `k` / `nghìn` / `ngàn` (x 1,000) and `tr` / `triệu` (x 1,000,000, with a decimal part: `1,5tr` = 1,500,000) after a number, keep the digit limit (`MAX_DIGITS`) on the result, and add the 7 spellings to `MoneyParserTest`. Do not combine with a `.` thousands group (`1.500k` stays refused). *(DONE in code, session 90: `MoneyParser.parseDong` reads `50k`, `50K`, `50 k`, `50 nghìn`/`ngàn`, `1tr`, `1 triệu`, `1,5tr`, `2,5k`, also followed by đ/VND, with an accent typed either way. The number before the word has no thousands separators and at most two decimals, so `1.500k`, `1,500k`, `1 000k`, `1,234tr` stay refused; the 15-digit limit holds on the result. The refusal text now gives `50k` and `1,5tr` as examples. 3 new tests in `MoneyParserTest` (14 pass in a plain JDK). It applies to every money box that uses `parseDong`: product price and cost, salary, CSV import, and the cash received / discount at the till.)*

On Windows each one gets its own taskbar button and a click on the main window puts it in front of them, so the receipt that just opened for a payment can end up hidden behind the till with nothing on screen to say it is still there (the cashier thinks the print did not open and pays or prints again). The windows are also not closed when the main window is, except on logout (2.30). **(confirmed in code that no owner is set; the stacking is the usual Windows behaviour, not seen here)** Pass the main stage as owner (`stage.initOwner(Session/main window)`), keep them non-modal, and test by hand on Windows: open a receipt, click the till, the receipt must stay in front.
   Done (session 106): `SceneNavigator.newOwnedStage()` creates every extra window with the main window as owner (not modal, so the till stays usable); the receipt window (`loadAndShowNewWindow`), `ReportWindow` and `OrderHistoryWindow` use it. An owned window stays in front of the till, is minimised and closed with it and has no taskbar button. `OwnedWindowsTest` fails the build if any other file writes a bare `new Stage()`. Only after it worked does `getConnection` also send `PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL` (safe with WAL, a durability risk without it). `reset()` clears the flag. Backups use `VACUUM INTO` (a plain file, tested by `aBackupOfAWalDatabaseIsStillAPlainValidFile`), the restore code deletes `-journal`, `-wal` and `-shm` before swapping the file, and the legacy-copy code refuses a folder that has a `-wal`. Docs (README, `docs/RELEASE.md`, `docs/DATABASE.md`): never copy `lunavera.db` by hand while the app is open, and delete `-wal`/`-shm` when putting a file back by hand. `DatabaseConfigWalTest` (4 tests). Caveat: WAL does not work on some network or synced folders (OneDrive, Dropbox); then the app logs a warning and stays in the normal mode. If anything fails on the first `mvn test`, look first at the two tests that compare the size of `lunavera.db` (`BackupSafetyTest`, `BackupServiceTest.aBrokenPendingRestoreFailsSafelyAndIsNotRetriedForever`): a checkpoint can change the main file size. 24. [x] **[Med] Add CHECK constraints to the schema.** **NEW (5th scan)** The tables have only `NOT NULL`/`UNIQUE`/foreign keys **(confirmed from the CREATE TABLE text)**. Nothing in the database refuses a negative price or stock, an order item with quantity 0 or less, a discount above the subtotal, or a status such as `'paid'` that no code understands. *(13th pass: **SQLite is done**: `SchemaMigrations.ensureDataRules` creates insert and update triggers that refuse a negative price, cost or stock, an order line with quantity 0 or less or negative money, an order with negative money or a discount above the subtotal, an unknown order status, an unknown user role or status, an unknown table status, and negative loyalty points. Triggers instead of `CHECK` because SQLite cannot add a `CHECK` to an existing table without rebuilding it, and one mechanism then covers new and old databases. The allowed values are written from the enums (`OrderStatus`, `Role`, `AccountStatus`, `TableStatus`), so they cannot drift. Safe at every start (`IF NOT EXISTS`); a rule is skipped, with a warning that gives only the rule and a row count plus the query that lists the rows, while rows already in the database break it (otherwise every later update of such a row would fail), and quietly when its table or column is missing. I read the code first so no real flow trips a rule: stock is only decreased with `stock >= quantity`, loyalty points are clamped at 0, a paid order is written in one statement that sets subtotal, discount and total together. The rules were run in Python's SQLite on the real `CREATE TABLE` text with a normal sale, a cancel, a restock and a loyalty clamp (all pass) and ten bad writes (all refused). `DataRulesTest` (9; NOT run, needs the driver). The MariaDB half was added in part 2 below; one rule is not a rule on either database: `total = subtotal - discount` for a paid order (the code checks it; a trigger would need to be sure no draft order breaks it).)* *(13th pass, part 2: DONE for both databases, so ticked. MariaDB/MySQL now gets the same rules as `CHECK` constraints: `SchemaMigrations.ensureMariaDataRules` runs at every start next to the staff-phone key, skips a rule that already exists (looked up in `information_schema.table_constraints`), skips a rule with a warning (rule name and row count only) while existing rows break it, and tries each rule on its own, so a failure (an old server that cannot add a `CHECK`, a lost connection) is logged and never stops the app from starting. The statement is `ALTER TABLE <t> ADD CONSTRAINT rule_<name> CHECK (NOT (<violation>))`, built by `mariaCheckSql`; `DataRuleSqlTest` (4, run here: pass) fixes its shape and checks that every column the rules name exists in both schemas. **NOT run on a MariaDB server**: the syntax is the documented one for MariaDB 10.2+ and MySQL 8.0.16+, and a rule that fails only produces a warning in the log, so look for \"Data rule ... was not added\" in `data/logs/` on the first start and, in phpMyAdmin, `SHOW CREATE TABLE products` should list `CONSTRAINT rule_products_not_negative CHECK ...`. A server older than MySQL 8.0.16 parses the `CHECK` and ignores it, and gets the statement again at each start. Still not a rule, on either database: `total = subtotal - discount` for a paid order (the code checks it).)*
        Add `CHECK (price >= 0)`, `CHECK (stock >= 0)`, `CHECK (quantity > 0)`, `CHECK (status IN ('OPEN','PAID','CANCELLED'))` and similar. SQLite cannot add a CHECK to an existing table, so this needs the "create new table, copy, drop, rename" migration (see 6.6), done with a backup first and tested on an old database.
