[x] **[Low] Run the MariaDB tests once with `sql_mode=ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY`.** **NEW (8th scan)** `ReportDAO.topProducts` selects `COALESCE(p.name, oi.product_name)` but groups by `COALESCE(CAST(oi.product_id AS CHAR), oi.product_name)`. SQLite and the default MariaDB mode accept it; MySQL, and a MariaDB server whose `sql_mode` includes `ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY`, refuse it with error 1055, and the report would fail on that shop's server. **(reasoned from the query; the 415 MariaDB test runs used the default mode)** Add the flag to the test server's settings and wrap the column in `MIN()`/`MAX()` or group by it. *(DONE in code, Session 131; **the proof is the CI job, not run here**: every `GROUP BY` in `src/main/java` was read (25 in 7 files: `ReportDAO` 7, `ShiftDAO` 1, `OrderDAO` 1, `CategoryDAO` 1, `CustomerDAO` 1, `TableDAO` 1, `SchemaMigrations` 13, two of which only show a query to the owner in a text). Only `ReportDAO.topProducts` selected a column it does not group by; its name column is now `MAX(COALESCE(p.name, oi.product_name))` (one product per group, so one name: the rows are the same). The others select exactly the grouped column or the same expression written the same, or put the rest in `MAX/SUM/COUNT`; no aggregate query mixes in a plain column without a `GROUP BY`. New `GroupByRulesTest` (3, reads sources only): the number of `GROUP BY` per file must equal the number read, so a new one fails the build until someone reads it (and says what was checked in the test's comment); `topProducts` keeps its `MAX`; the `test-mariadb` job of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` must switch `ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY` on before the tests. That job now has the step "Turn ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY on in the test server": `docker exec` into the MariaDB service container and `SET GLOBAL sql_mode = CONCAT(@@GLOBAL.sql_mode, ',ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY')`. Checked here: old and new `topProducts` text run on SQLite (Python) over a renamed product, two products with one name, a deleted product with two old names, a line without cost and a cancelled order: identical rows for every limit; the 14 tests of `Section4ReportsTest` give the same result with the old and the new `ReportDAO` on the real `sqlite-jdbc` (4 of them fail with the stand-in runner both before and after, so they are about the runner or the shared test data, not this change). Not checked: a real MariaDB or MySQL server; whether MariaDB accepts a selected expression that equals the grouped expression (`dailySales`, `employeeSales`, `ShiftDAO.summarize`) is expected from its rules, not seen: if the CI job fails on one of them, wrap that column in `MAX()` like `topProducts`. The docker command in the CI step is unproved too.)*

[x] **[Low] `QrCodeTest` checks one symbol of version 1; the other 39 versions are untested.** **NEW (16th scan)** `matchesIndependentReferenceEncoder` compares one text (version 1, 21x21) with stored modules; the other tests check sizes, finder patterns and timing. Alignment patterns (version 2 and up), the version information (7 and up) and the interleaving of several Reed-Solomon blocks (version 5 and up) are never looked at, and the receipt QR switches between versions with the length of the text. **To settle it I decoded the output independently:** 86 texts of 1 to 2,331 bytes (every capacity boundary of the table, one real receipt text, one Vietnamese text) were encoded and then read back by a separate Python decoder (format information and its BCH check, both copies; mask; zig-zag read; de-interleaving; Reed-Solomon check of every block; byte-mode payload). **All 86 verified, in all 40 versions, and the two ECC tables equal the standard ones** - so the encoder is right today; nothing protects it from a change. Add a test-side `QrDecoder` (about 120 lines: the same steps) and a test that round-trips a text of each version-changing length, plus one with a text of more than 255 bytes (16-bit count field from version 10). Keep it in `src/test` only.
   Done (session 106): a test-side `QrDecoder` (`src/test`, ~250 lines, own tables for capacity, error-correction blocks and alignment centres) reads the encoder's symbols back: both format copies and their BCH check, level M, mask, both version-information copies (7 and up), zig-zag read, de-interleaving, Reed-Solomon check of every block, byte-mode header with the 8-bit/16-bit count, terminator and padding. `QrCodeAllVersionsTest` round-trips the largest text of every one of the 40 versions and the shortest text of each (capacity boundary +1), an empty text, the 2,331-byte maximum, texts over 255 bytes, Vietnamese text growing through 8+ versions, a transfer note, and all 8 masks at versions 1, 2, 5, 7, 10, 14, 25, 40; the decoder is also shown to refuse a damaged module, format bit and version bit. Ran here (plain JDK): 10 of 10 pass, and four planted encoder bugs (alignment step, version bits, count-field cut-over, block table) each made the tests fail.

The folder holds the data folder with the starter menu photos copied into it, about 3 MB. **Reproduced:** about a hundred runs of my test and probe programs left about a hundred such folders (300 MB) in `/tmp` until the sandbox disk was full; with Maven it is one folder per `mvn test`, which `mvn clean` does not remove (it is outside `target/`) and which on Windows piles up in `%TEMP%`. Fix: register a shutdown hook that deletes `TEMP_HOME` recursively, or create it under `target/` (where `pom.xml` already points `lunavera.dataDir` and `user.home`, a setting this class overrides). *(DONE in code, Session 97: `TestDatabaseSupport` registers a shutdown hook that deletes `TEMP_HOME` recursively, best effort. Other small test folders (`qrguard`, `lunavera-ui`, ...) are still left in the temp folder; they are a few KB.)*

[x] **[Low] `CrashHandler`'s one-dialog-per-10-seconds rule reads the computer's clock.** **NEW (session 84)** `CrashHandler.report` passes `System.currentTimeMillis()` to `shouldShowDialog`, with `lastDialogMillis = 0` as "never shown". With the clock set back by a minute after a dialog, error dialogs stop for a minute; set forward, a burst of errors shows a dialog every time. **(confirmed in code; the same family as 2A.37, which fixed the login delay, the idle logout and the log limiter)** Pass `MonotonicClock.millis()` and replace the `0` with a real "never shown yet" state (a `boolean` or `Long.MIN_VALUE` guarded by a check), because a monotonic clock's zero is arbitrary and can be negative, so `now - 0 < 10_000` could suppress the very first dialog. `CrashHandlerTest` already passes the time in; add a negative-clock case. *(DONE in code, Session 96: `CrashHandler.report` passes `MonotonicClock.millis()`, and "never shown yet" is now its own `boolean` (`dialogShown`) instead of the value 0, so a negative or zero reading can neither hide the very first dialog nor break the 10-second spacing; `shouldShowDialog` uses only the difference of two readings. New tests `aNegativeClockStillShowsTheFirstDialogAndSpacesTheRest` and `aReadingOfZeroIsNotMistakenForNeverShown`; all 3 tests of `CrashHandlerTest` passed here with a stand-in runner (JavaFX `Platform` and `AlertUtil` replaced by empty stubs), and the two new ones fail on the old logic. Not run under Maven.)*

[x] **[Med] The CI never runs the tests on MariaDB, which is the shop's default database.** **NEW (session 84)** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `./mvnw -B test` on Ubuntu and Windows with SQLite only (its first comment says so: "Tests use SQLite, no database server needed"; there is no `services:` block and the word MariaDB does not appear in the file). The MariaDB code (`MariaDbSchema`, `MariaDbMigrations`, `SqlDialect`, the data rules, the importer, the mysqldump backup) is only exercised when somebody starts a server by hand and runs `mvn test -Dlunavera.test.db=mariadb -Dlunavera.test.db.password=...` (`docs/DATABASE.md`, `TestDatabaseSupport.configureMariaDbIfRequested`), so a change that breaks MariaDB leaves the CI green. **(confirmed from the workflow file and the test support class)** Add a second job on Ubuntu with a service container and the same Maven command the guide uses:
       ```yaml
       test-mariadb:
         runs-on: ubuntu-latest
         services:
           mariadb:
             image: mariadb:10.11
             env: { MARIADB_DATABASE: lunavera_test, MARIADB_USER: lunavera_test, MARIADB_PASSWORD: test-password, MARIADB_ROOT_PASSWORD: root-test-password }
             ports: ['3306:3306']
             options: >-
               --health-cmd="healthcheck.sh --connect --innodb_initialized" --health-interval=5s --health-timeout=5s --health-retries=20
         steps:  # same checkout and setup-java steps as the test job, then:
           - run: ./mvnw -B test -Dlunavera.test.db=mariadb -Dlunavera.test.db.password=test-password
       ```
       The database name ends in `_test` as the support class requires (it drops every table first and refuses any other name), the host and port are the defaults it expects, and the tests that are about SQLite files are skipped on their own (`requireSqlite`). Make the job a required check once it is green; the number of tests Maven prints will be lower than on SQLite. Do this together with 6.51 (the pins and `permissions:` are done; `timeout-minutes` is still open). Not run here: GitHub is not reachable and there is no MariaDB server in this sandbox, so the YAML above comes from the image's documented variables and the test class, not from a run. **DONE in the workflow (Session 109):** the job `test-mariadb` is in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` as above, with the same pinned `checkout` and `setup-java` as the `test` job, `timeout-minutes: 30` and the surefire reports uploaded when it fails; `docs/DATABASE.md` section 10 says the CI runs it. The YAML parses; it has **not run on GitHub**, so the first push may fail for reasons only a real server shows (first suspect: a test that is not skipped in MariaDB mode but needs SQLite). By hand: make `test-mariadb` a required check once it is green.

## 8. Done (for reference)
Quality of life (cleanup doc, section 4) items 1-10 are done. Items 6-9 (log no longer prints the default password, LIKE escaping in the order
history search, `orders.table_number` in the CREATE TABLE plus a checked add for old databases, the `LoginBackground.png` rename) were done after items 1-5;
see progress.md Session 33. Cleanup list steps 1-3 (dead code, unused dependencies, bugs) are done as well.

**Checked in the 3rd scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- No SQL is built from user input: every value goes through a `PreparedStatement` parameter. The few concatenated statements use fixed table names or ids read from the database itself.
- Password verification compares the hashes in constant time, a nonexistent username costs one hash, and the locked-account message only appears after the right password.
- Money is stored as whole dong (integer), foreign keys are on, and each multi-step write (payment, cancel, cleanup) runs in one transaction.
- The report window and the backup button check for an admin account; cancelling a paid order is admin-only.
- Passwords are never written to the log (the default admin password line was removed).

**Checked in the 4th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- User-entered text never reaches a file path, an FXML file or a style sheet; the export file name is built from dates only.
- The report window is admin-only and the account lock refuses to lock the signed-in account (the last-admin race is item 2A.2).
- The report export uses a BOM and `\r\n`, so Excel shows Vietnamese correctly.

**Checked in the 5th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- `VACUUM INTO` gets its path with single quotes doubled, so a folder name with an apostrophe cannot break or inject into the statement (the other problems with `backupTo` are bug 2.23).
- Payment is guarded against double payment: `payExistingOrder` only updates a row that is still `OPEN`, and a second attempt gets a clear message and changes nothing. Stock cannot go below zero (`stock >= ?` in the same transaction).
- Cancelling a paid order restocks, takes back points and changes the status in one transaction and rolls back on any error.
- The idle `Async` threads are daemons, the ordered queue is one thread (autosave then payment cannot overtake each other), and the ordered-queue name in the code is `runOrdered`.
- No log line I found writes a phone number, a name or a password.
- Not scanned in depth in this round (so no claim either way): the CSS files, the layout of every FXML file, `OrderHistoryWindow` and `ReportWindow` beyond what earlier scans covered.


**Checked in the 6th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- Every `onAction`/`onMouse...="#handler"` in the nine FXML files has a matching method in its controller (0 missing); every `<stylesheets>` and image path in the FXML files points to a file that exists.
- Deleting a product that has sales is blocked by the screen with a clear offer to stop selling it instead (`countOrderReferences`); the DAO alone would fail with a foreign-key error (reproduced: "Failed to delete product"), which is the intended last defence.
- Customer phone numbers are normalised inside `CustomerDAO` (insert, update, `existsByPhone`): `+84912345678` was stored as `0912345678`, so the customer dialog cannot create the same person under two spellings.
- The login screen runs the password check off the UI thread, trims only the username (not the password) and shows one message for a wrong name or password.
- The history search escapes `%` and `_`, sorting and paging happen in SQL, and the cancel button is re-checked for admin on click (not only when the row is selected).
- The report export writes a BOM and `\r\n` and shows the saved path; the report and its totals are admin-only.
- What I could not do: view any screen (no display here), so the visual look of every FXML/CSS screen, the 1366x768 fit, and the Windows glyphs remain for the human checks in section 0.


**Checked in the 7th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- Restore cannot write outside the pictures folder: `extractImages` (called `replaceImages` before the 2.42 fix) normalises every zip entry path and skips one that does not start with `images/`. *(25th scan: re-read, the check `file.startsWith(root)` is there.)*
- A backup is written to `<name>.zip.part` and moved into place at the end, always ends in `.zip` (so it can never be `lunavera.db`), and automatic pruning touches only `auto-*.zip`; this matches what 2.8 says.
- Every SQL value in the DAOs I read (users, orders, customers, products, employees, tables, report) goes through a `PreparedStatement` parameter; the history `ORDER BY` is a whitelist; the few concatenated statements use fixed names or ids read from the database.
- Login: a nonexistent username costs one hash (`dummyHash`), a locked account is reported only after the right password, and `PasswordChangeService` checks the current password before a voluntary change.
- Payment: the discount rule is checked in the dialog and again by `DiscountPolicy`, a transfer payment cannot be completed without the tick box, and stock cannot go below zero inside the payment transaction.
- Idle logout closes every other window and the forced password dialog (hiding it ends the session cleanly).
- Read in a second round: `CategoryDAO`, `MenuSeeder`, `ScreenFit`, `CustomerController`'s dialog. Still not scanned in depth: the CSS and FXML layouts, `QrCode` internals, `MenuSeedData`, `PagedTable`/`PageBar`/`DashboardWidgets`, and the content of the tests (only their names were compared with the classes).
